## Session — 2026-07-28 (Pipeline root-fix: two run_all_tasks tasks were failing EVERY cycle)

Found by tallying real task outcomes from the live logs rather than trusting "the pipeline
is running". `run_all_tasks` completed cycles, but two of its tasks errored on every single
run and the loop logged the error and moved on — so the pipeline was *running* but not
*working*, and the category backfill had been competing with them for row locks (39 rows/s).

| Item | Status | Detail |
|------|--------|--------|
| **`normalize_categories` — `function max(uuid) does not exist`** | ✅ 2026-07-28 | Pass 2 did `SELECT MAX(id) FROM parts_catalog` to pin a cutoff. `id` is a **uuid** and Postgres has no `max(uuid)` aggregate, so the task raised on every cycle. Fixed with `ORDER BY id DESC LIMIT 1` (same value, uses the PK index). |
| **`normalize_categories` — pass 2 scanned >1M rows to find zero** | ✅ 2026-07-28 | After the category backfill set `updated_at=NOW()` on 1.2M rows, pass 2's `updated_at > :since AND TRIM(category) NOT IN (…23 values…) ORDER BY id` scanned the whole delta and timed out — while the correct answer was "there is nothing to do" (0 non-canonical values). Now resolves the concrete bad values via a cheap `DISTINCT` on the indexed column first and **skips the pass entirely** when the list is empty; when non-empty it batches on an index-usable `category = ANY(:bad_cats)`. |
| **`normalize_part_types` — three separate faults, each hiding the next** | ✅ 2026-07-28 | **(1)** predicate was `LOWER(TRIM(part_type)) = ANY(:keys)` — a function on the column, so no index could serve it (classic "scan-for-nothing"). **(2)** After switching to exact values, `PART_TYPE_MAP` maps `'oem'→'OEM'` and `'aftermarket'→'Aftermarket'` while `parts_catalog` stores **3.28M `'oem'` + 1.09M `'aftermarket'`** lowercase — so the task tried to case-change **4.3M rows** every cycle, which has never once completed. A case-only difference is NOT a defect (nothing reads `part_type` case-sensitively), so it is now skipped and the task converges on genuine garbage only (~60 rows in parts_catalog, ~32K in supplier_parts). **(3)** `ORDER BY id` forced a **Sort** (EXPLAIN cost 79,218) because `id` is not in `idx_supplier_parts_part_type_part_id`; ordering on the index columns (`part_type, part_id`) is a pure **Index Scan** (startup cost 0.43). Verified live: bad rows drained 31,882 → 11,874 → 0. |
| **NOT normalising 4.3M rows to the capitalised form** | ⚠️ owner decision | `parts_catalog` uses lowercase `part_type`, `supplier_parts` uses capitalised. The two tables have genuinely diverged. Picking a winner across 4.3M rows is a data-policy decision, not a side effect of a bug fix — deliberately left alone and raised to the owner. |

**Rules reinforced:** (1) *"the loop is running" is not "the tasks are succeeding"* — tally per-task `status=` from the logs, don't infer health from cycle completion. (2) An `ORDER BY` that doesn't match the index turns an index scan into a sort over the whole match set — always `EXPLAIN` a batch query that times out before assuming the predicate is the problem. (3) Before mass-rewriting a column, check whether the "wrong" value is actually the established convention.

## Session — 2026-07-27c (Category system: ONE file, ONE vocabulary — full merge)

Supersedes and corrects the 2026-07-27b entry below, which recorded "3 separate category
rule sets". A full inventory found **14 independent rule sets across 3 incompatible
vocabularies** — the earlier entry fixed the symptom in 3 of them.

| Item | Status | Detail |
|------|--------|--------|
| **ROOT CAUSE: three incompatible category VOCABULARIES, not just duplicate rules** | ✅ 2026-07-27 | (A) English slugs — `brakes`, `body-exterior`, `כללי` — what `parts_catalog.category` actually stores. (B) Hebrew display names — `בלמים`, `מערכת דלק` — the targets of `db_update_agent.CATEGORY_MAP`. (C) 131 Title-Case labels — `Air Filters`, `Timing Belts` — `categories.CATEGORY_MAP.keys()`, used as `db_update_agent.CANONICAL_CATEGORIES`. `normalize_categories` mapped raw→**B** but only kept CASE branches whose target was in **C**. A Hebrew string is never in an English-label set, so **nearly every mapping branch was silently discarded**; the only survivors were `general|misc|other|אחר → כללי`. **That is the mechanism by which parts flowed INTO the catch-all and never left.** Fix: vocabulary A is the truth; B is now display-only (`category_map.DISPLAY`, never stored); C is folded into `VARIANT_MAP` as input. |
| **14 private rule sets merged into `category_map.py`** | ✅ 2026-07-27 | Removed from: `categories.py` (`_HEBREW_SUPPLEMENT`, ~200 phrases), `db_update_agent.py` (`CATEGORY_MAP` + `CATEGORY_NAME_REMAP`), `routes/parts.py` (`FILTER_CATEGORY_MAP` + `CANONICAL_FILTER_CATEGORIES` + `FILTER_CATEGORY_KEYWORDS`), `oempartsonline_importer.py`, `multi_brand_seed_import.py`, `geely_seed_import.py`, `fridayparts_seed_import.py`, `gm_brands_seed_import.py`, `zeekr_full_import.py`, `cadillac_israel_import.py`, `gmc_buick_umi_import.py`, `saab_partsforsaabs_importer.py`, `selected_parts_scraper.py`, `bmw_spare_parts_scraper.py`, `samelet_import.py`, `samelet_import_v2.py`, `lr_import.py`, `subaru_il_importer.py`, `eliteparts_scraper.py`, `ebay_brand_importer.py`, `kgm_ssangyong_importer.py`, `rockauto_browser_import.py`, `car_parts_ie_import_generic.py` (dead duplicate `CATEGORY_SLUG_MAP`). ~33K characters of divergent rules deleted. `categories.py` is now a thin deprecated re-export shim. |
| **Importers were writing values that are not categories at all** | ✅ 2026-07-27 | `samelet_import.py`/`samelet_import_v2.py` → `"General Parts"`; `lr_import.py` → `"Auto Parts"`; `subaru_il_importer.py` → `"Other Parts"`; `eliteparts_scraper.py` → `"Engine"` (capitalised, AND used as the catch-all); `zeekr_full_import.py` → `"Service & General"`; `bmw_spare_parts_scraper.py` → display labels (`"Fuel & Air"`, `"Engine Cooling"`) with fallback `"Engine"`; `ai_catalog_builder.py` `DEFAULT_CATEGORY = "כלי עבודה ואביזרים"`. None are storable values — every part they classified got an unusable label. All now return canonical slugs via `categorize_on_ingest()`. |
| **Real errors inside the private importer maps** | ✅ 2026-07-27 | `"filters" → "engine"` (filters is its own category), `"bearing" → "engine"` (→ wheels-bearings), `"turbocharging" → "engine"` (→ fuel-air), `"interior accessories" → "interior"` (not a canonical value at all), `'מסנן'/'פילטר'/'אוויר' → 'engine'` in the Cadillac/GMC rules (bare `'אוויר'`/air matched almost anything). Fixed centrally — one map, one set of targets. |
| **`catalog_scraper._guess_category_from_text` fell back to `"accessories"`** | ✅ 2026-07-27 | Every scraped part whose name matched no keyword was filed as an accessory — a large share of the 121,649-row `accessories` bucket. Now falls back to `CATCH_ALL`. |
| **SEARCH API category filter was comparing against values the column never holds** | ✅ 2026-07-27 | `routes/parts.py::_normalize_filter_category` mapped user input to Hebrew display names (`"brakes" → "בלמים"`) and validated against a 14-entry Hebrew `CANONICAL_FILTER_CATEGORIES`, while the column stores English slugs. Facet buckets and keyword expansion were keyed the same way. Re-keyed to canonical slugs; verified `brakes`/`בלמים`/`Brakes`/`فرامل`/`oil filter`/`tyres` all resolve correctly. |
| **Matching changed to LONGEST-KEYWORD-WINS** | ✅ 2026-07-27 | Previously the first category whose keyword list hit won, so a generic word in an early category shadowed a specific phrase in a later one (`'שמן'`→fluids beat `'מסנן שמן'`→filters; `'בולם'`→suspension beat `'בולם הגה'`). `_FLAT_RULES` is now sorted by keyword length DESC (stable, so RULES order only breaks ties). This removed the whole ordering-fragility class of bug — **and made it safe to add bare head-nouns**, which was the single biggest coverage gap (see below). |
| **Word-boundary matching for short RTL keywords** | ✅ 2026-07-27 | Hebrew/Arabic are matched as substrings, so a 2-3 letter keyword fired inside unrelated longer words: `'לד'` (LED) matched inside `'לדשבורט'` ("for the dashboard") and filed a dashboard bracket under `lighting` — found in live rows. Short RTL keywords now use a pattern that allows a Hebrew one-letter proclitic (ה ו ב ל מ ש כ) before the word but forbids another RTL letter after it, so `'לפנס'`/`'הפנס'` still match while `'לדשבורט'` does not. |
| **RTL-in-`name` rule (the "name rule")** | ✅ 2026-07-27 verified | 124,615 parts in the bad buckets have Hebrew/Arabic text in `name` with an EMPTY `name_he`; RTL keywords were only ever matched against `name_he`, so none of them could match. Measured on 4,000 real rows: **0.1% → 63.5% categorized**. |
| **Arabic coverage** | ✅ 2026-07-27 verified | 234 Arabic keywords across all 22 real categories (every category audited, none empty) + Arabic display names in `DISPLAY`. Verified 22/22 on real Arabic part names (`فلتر زيت المحرك`→filters, `ممتص الصدمات`→suspension-steering, `وسادة هوائية`→safety-systems, …). |
| **Coverage gap was BARE HEAD-NOUNS, found by token analysis not guesswork** | ✅ 2026-07-27 | Token-frequency analysis of 24,845 real unmatched rows showed the rules held plenty of specific phrases but no bare nouns: `engine` appeared 765×, `door` 739×, `belt` 505×, `brake` 338× with no rule to catch them. Added a clearly-marked lowest-precedence GENERIC tier (safe only because of longest-match-wins). Also fixed catalog truncations (`"Cover-cushio"`), reversed word order (`"absorber assembly shock"`), a Hebrew spelling variant (`חגורת ביטחון` vs `בטיחות`), and `"heatshield"` as one word. |
| **Bare fasteners deliberately NOT guessed** | ✅ by design | `bolt`/`screw`/`nut`/`washer`/`shim`/`בורג` on their own have no category, and a wrong category is worse than `כללי`. They stay in the catch-all. **In context they still classify** — `"Bolt Cylinder Head"` matches the longer `cylinder head` → engine. This is ~18% of the residual catch-all. |
| **Non-parts routed to `accessories`** | ✅ 2026-07-27 | Suppliers list workwear and printed documentation alongside parts (`"Modyf Trousers Natur"`, `"Owner S Manual For F30"`, `"Drivers Manual Macan"`). These are not vehicle parts but are not unknown either — mapped to `accessories`. |
| **Measured result on a real random live sample (20,000 backlog parts)** | ✅ 2026-07-27 | **36.8% → 73.6% categorized**; `כללי` 63.2% → 26.4%. **Zero non-canonical outputs.** 15/15 specific-phrase regression tests pass (a generic never outranks a specific phrase). |
| **Scan scope + LLM allowlist derived from `category_map`, not hardcoded** | ✅ 2026-07-27 | `db_cleanup_agent` task3 and `categorize_parts_batch.py` both build their WHERE from `BAD_FALLBACK_BUCKETS` **plus `category NOT IN CANONICAL`** — so non-canonical junk (`'Brakes'`, `'General Parts'`, `'tools-equipment'`) is now in scope and the scope can never drift from the rule file. `_VALID_CATEGORIES` (LLM fallback allowlist) derived from `CANONICAL`: was missing `safety-systems`, `belts-chains`, `hybrid-ev`, so the LLM could never choose them. |
| **task3 in-RAM index bounded** | ✅ 2026-07-27 | `_build_uncategorized_index` loaded EVERY matching row into a dict inside the uvicorn process — 1,617,345 rows, several hundred MB in a 4 GB container that also serves the API and the harvester supervisors. task3 only samples 500 ids per pass, so the window is now capped (`CLEANUP_INDEX_LIMIT`, default 50,000) and simply rebuilds more often. |
| **Duplicate categorizer scripts archived** | ✅ 2026-07-27 | `maintenance/categorize_kllali_parts.py` and `maintenance/categorize_single_pass.py` each carried a third and fourth full RULES array and were superseded by `categorize_parts_batch.py`. Moved to `legacy/`; confirmed nothing references them. |
| **Backfill loop would never drain — rows moved to `כללי` stayed in scope** | ✅ 2026-07-27 | `כללי` is BOTH a scope member (old catch-all rows must be retried against the new rules) AND the destination for anything still unmatched, so every row the pass wrote was immediately re-eligible and got re-fetched forever. Exactly the "perpetually refreshes rows back into scope and never terminates" trap already in CLAUDE.md. Fix: pin `run_start = LOCALTIMESTAMP` and claim only `updated_at < run_start` — every write sets `updated_at = NOW()`, so each row is considered exactly once per run. |
| **Backfill claim query was a full seq scan on every batch (~50 rows/s)** | ✅ 2026-07-27 | `... OR category NOT IN (23 canonical values)` is not index-usable, so each 5,000-row batch scanned 4.3M rows (measured 108s and 177s for batches 1 and 2). Fix: resolve the open-ended "not canonical" half ONCE at run start via a cheap `GROUP BY category` (indexed), then claim with a plain `category = ANY($3)`. Measured claim time after the fix: **45 ms**. Note: the column must NOT be wrapped in `COALESCE`/`TRIM` — a function on the column makes `ix_parts_catalog_category` unusable (verified 0 NULL / 0 empty categories, so no wrapper is needed). |
| **`SELECT NOW()` would have crashed the backfill at runtime** | ✅ 2026-07-27 | `NOW()` returns a tz-AWARE timestamp but `parts_catalog.updated_at` is `timestamp WITHOUT time zone`; asyncpg refuses to bind one against the other (`can't subtract offset-naive and offset-aware datetimes`). This is the SAME trap already recorded in the mistake log for `normalize_part_types`/`normalize_categories`/`dedup` — caught here before the run, by testing the query rather than trusting it. Use `LOCALTIMESTAMP`. |
| **LLM assist re-enabled as a HELPER for stuck workers (owner directive)** | ✅ 2026-07-27 verified | The owner's framing: *"use the llm only if the workers got stuck and don't know what to do — the llm here will be a kind of helper and guider, not the main tool to do the worker tasks."* Implemented exactly that. `db_cleanup_agent.task3b` now only sees rows the deterministic matcher genuinely could not place, in budgeted batches, and **every answer is mined back into permanent keyword rules** (`category_learning.py` + `category_learned_keywords`), so the matcher handles that shape of name unaided next time and LLM demand SHRINKS. New file `category_learning.py`; `category_map` gained `LEARNED` / `register_learned_keywords()` / `learned_stats()` and stays DB-free (it is imported by every importer, scraper and the API). Learned keywords are rehydrated at cleanup-loop startup so learning is cumulative across restarts. |
| **Quota-blowout shape made structurally impossible** | ✅ 2026-07-27 | The 2026-07-27 incident was not cost, it was an unbounded caller: `task3b(batch_size=500)` inside a 30s loop, with nothing learned, so demand was constant and infinite. Three independent limits now: `CLEANUP_LLM_BATCH=25` (call site no longer passes an override at all), `CLEANUP_LLM_MIN_INTERVAL_S=180`, `CLEANUP_LLM_DAILY_MAX_CALLS=150`, plus provider-failure backoff — all in `docker-compose.yml`, enforced by `_llm_budget_check()`. |
| **Learning safety gates — all three verified live** | ✅ 2026-07-27 | (1) **A learned keyword can never override a hand-written rule**: `_covered_by_handwritten()` rejects it, and the stable length-sort keeps hand-written first on a tie — proved by feeding 4 votes for `brake→cooling` and confirming `"brake pad" → brakes` still holds. (2) **Consensus accumulates ACROSS calls** (`MIN_CONSENSUS=3`, `MIN_AGREEMENT=0.8`) — the table stores one row per (token, category) VOTE, because a 25-part batch almost never repeats a token and gating inside one batch would learn nothing forever; proved by activating a fictional token on the 3rd batch, not the 1st or 2nd. (3) **Conflicting votes never activate** — engine/brakes/lighting ×1 each stays `כללי`. Also, the LLM may only choose from `CANONICAL` (`_VALID_CATEGORIES` is derived from it). |
| **BACKFILL COMPLETE — 1,194,809 parts recategorized** | ✅ 2026-07-27 verified | 3 workers, `FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED`, ~7,070s. **`general` 775,028 → 0** · **`tools-equipment` 148 → 0** · `service-general` 591,711 → 11,757 (only genuine service kits) · `accessories` 121,232 → 8,203 (only genuine accessories) · `כללי` 128,651 → 403,442 (the honest residual). **Zero non-canonical values remain in the column.** Biggest gainers: body-exterior +379,924, electrical-sensors +140,208, engine +124,490, suspension-steering +94,611, wheels-bearings +80,361. The 403,442 residual matches the predicted 26.4% almost exactly, and is dominated by bare fasteners (deliberately not guessed) and brand-name-only rows — that population is what the LLM assist now works through, three keywords at a time. |
| **LLM-assist throughput root-fixed: 107 days → days** | ✅ 2026-07-27 measured | The owner flagged that per-part LLM classification would take forever. Correct: 25 parts/call × 150 calls/day = 3,750/day → **107 days** for 403,327 stuck parts. But the unit of work was wrong. Token analysis of the REAL stuck population shows the **top 400 unknown tokens cover 73.6% (~297,000 parts)** — `bolt` appears in 16,541, `rover` 13,068, `washer` 10,444. Two fixes: **(1) highest-leverage sampling** — task3b now ranks unknown tokens by frequency and samples parts containing the most common one, so every expensive call teaches a high-value rule instead of 25 random anecdotes; **(2) `_bulk_apply_new_keywords()`** — the moment a keyword activates, the matcher is re-run over the catch-all bucket so that one keyword fixes thousands of parts immediately, not just the 25 that taught it. |
| **CRITICAL: blocklist filtered only at write-time — pre-existing votes still activated** | ✅ 2026-07-27 caught live | The owner asked what happens if the LLM is consistently wrong. It happened for real within one cycle: `bolt` and `washer` activated as `service-general`, and `rover`/`land` were one vote short of filing ~26,000 Land Rover parts under one category. Cause: `is_blocked()` ran in `mine_tokens()` (producer) but not `load_into_matcher()` (consumer), so rows written before the blocklist existed bypassed it on the next boot. **Fix: enforce at load time too + `purge_blocklisted()` at startup.** Verified at all three layers including a direct DB insert of 9 observations that bypasses both filters — still ignored. 14 tokens purged, **142 mis-filed parts reverted, `service-general` restored to exactly its post-backfill 11,757**. |
| **NEVER-LEARN blocklist (brands loaded live from the DB)** | ✅ 2026-07-27 verified | Four classes the LLM must never turn into a category rule: **brand names** (164 entries built live from `car_brands` + `parts_catalog.manufacturer`, split on spaces AND hyphens so "Mercedes-Benz" blocks both `mercedes` and `benz`), **bare fasteners** (`bolt`/`washer`/`screw`/`בורג`/`shim`/`ring`/`nut`), **position words** (`front`/`rear`/`קד`/`אח`/`ימין` — they say WHERE a part sits, not what it is), and **size/quantity codes** (anything containing a digit, `xxl`, `pcs`). Legitimate part words (`bezel`, `squab`, `ashtray`, `plenum`, `cowl`, `gaiter`) verified still allowed. |
| **Rules expanded for the words the LLM was getting wrong** | ✅ 2026-07-27 | Rather than let the LLM keep guessing at them: `checkarm`/`check arm` (a DOOR CHECK — the LLM said brakes), `blind`/`sunshade` (interior, not safety-systems), `ashtray`, `bezel`, `insulator`, `garnish`, `weatherstrip`, `escutcheon`, `wheelhouse`, `scuff plate`, `kick panel`, plus cooling `water/coolant/cooler line`. 1,571 → 1,620 hand-written keywords, **zero regressions** on the specific-phrase suite. |
| **Owner approval gate for learned keywords (`מילים` console command)** | ✅ 2026-07-27 verified | A learned keyword can move thousands of parts, so it no longer activates on its own. Lifecycle `pending → approved | rejected`; only `approved` rows load into the matcher, rejections persist across restarts (`load_rejected`) and are never re-proposed. WhatsApp console: **`מילים`** (list) · **`אשרמילה <word>`** (approve → goes live + bulk-applies) · **`דחהמילה <word>`** (reject). Owner pinged once/day via `_wa_send_quiet` (quiet-hours-safe, Redis `nx` dedupe) when tokens await review. Tested live end-to-end: list → approve → reject → blocklisted-token refusal all return correct Hebrew replies. |
| **Verification method** | ✅ 2026-07-27 | AST-based check in the live container: every import-safe module imports; all 91 `from category_map import X` symbols resolve; **no file outside `category_map.py`/`categories.py` defines a module-level category rule set**. Script modules that do work at import (`lr_import.py`) are `py_compile`d, never imported — per the CLAUDE.md rule. |

## Session — 2026-07-27b (Category pipeline merge + AI API rate-limit root fix)

| Item | Status | Detail |
|------|--------|--------|
| **ROOT CAUSE: 3 separate category rule sets caused 1.67M parts stuck in wrong buckets** | ✅ 2026-07-27 | Three separate RULES arrays existed: `category_map.py`, `maintenance/categorize_parts_batch.py` (its own copy), and `categories.py`/`part_type_taxonomy.py`. None were in sync. The batch script's rules were richer but silently diverged from category_map.py's rules. **Fix: merged into ONE source of truth** — `category_map.py` now has the comprehensive dual-array (HE+EN) RULES format from categorize_parts_batch.py + VARIANT_MAP + CATEGORY_SLUG_MAP + `categorize_on_ingest()` + `categorize_slug()`. `categorize_parts_batch.py` now imports from category_map.py (no local rules). `db_cleanup_agent.py` already delegated to category_map.categorize(). |
| **BUG: `categorize_parts_batch.py` line 258 moved unmatched parts to `'general'` instead of `'כללי'`** | ✅ 2026-07-27 | The July 13 batch run created the entire 865K 'general' backlog with this one line: `category='general'`. Changed to `category='כללי'`. The batch now processes ALL non-canonical buckets: `('כללי', 'general', 'service-general', 'accessories')`. |
| **BUG: `_build_uncategorized_index` WHERE clause only matched `category='כללי'` — 0 rows in scope** | ✅ 2026-07-27 | `db_cleanup_agent.py` task3's in-memory index was built from parts where `category = 'כללי'` only — so the entire 865K 'general' + 663K 'service-general' + 142K 'accessories' backlog was invisible to the cleanup loop. WHERE expanded to: `category IN ('כללי', 'general', 'service-general', 'accessories')`. |
| **BUG: `categorize()` returned `None` for unmatched parts — importers had to provide their own fallback** | ✅ 2026-07-27 | Every importer provided its own fallback ('service-general', 'accessories', 'general') because `categorize()` returned None. Changed `categorize()` to always return 'כללי' as the last resort. Added `categorize_on_ingest()` as a named function for clarity. No importer should ever need to hard-code a fallback category again. |
| **BUG: 12 importers used 'service-general' or 'accessories' as hard-coded fallback** | ✅ 2026-07-27 | All changed to 'כללי': `car_parts_ie_import_generic.py`, `oempartsonline_importer.py`, `ebay_brand_importer.py`, `opel_car_parts_ie_import.py`, `cadillac_israel_import.py`, `rockauto_browser_import.py`, `gmc_buick_umi_import.py` (both sites), `saab_partsforsaabs_importer.py`, `kgm_ssangyong_importer.py`, `gm_brands_seed_import.py`, `fridayparts_seed_import.py`, `geely_seed_import.py`, `multi_brand_seed_import.py`. `_map_category()` in car_parts_ie now delegates entirely to `categorize_slug()` from category_map.py. |
| **BUG: `normalize_categories` stuck checkpoint at 2026-07-03 → timeout death spiral** | ✅ 2026-07-27 | Checkpoint was never updated because the task scanned 24+ days of delta rows, hit the 60-min timeout, failed to save checkpoint, and repeated forever. Reset checkpoint to `NOW() - 2h` in `system_settings` so the task only processes recent rows and completes in time to save progress. |
| **ROOT CAUSE: AI API rate exhaustion from cron loops (Cerebras/Gemini/Groq simultaneously depleted)** | ✅ 2026-07-27 | `task3b_llm_category_fallback(batch_size=500)` was firing 500 Cerebras calls every 30 seconds = 60K tokens/min (the entire free-tier TPM budget, continuously). `lookup_oem_spec(limit=500)` was 500 sequential Cerebras calls per 3h cycle. `enrich_pending_parts` was double-called (30-min loop AND run_all_tasks). **Fix:** added three env-variable gates — all OFF by default: `CLEANUP_LLM_ENABLED` (task3b in cleanup loop, default 0), `OEM_LOOKUP_ENABLED` (lookup_oem_spec in run_all_tasks, default 0), `ENRICH_PARTS_ENABLED` (enrich_pending_parts in both loops, default 0). LLMs belong in on-demand customer chat, NOT in background cron loops that burn quota continuously. |
| **Backfill of 1.67M wrongly-categorized parts — RUNNING** | ⏳ 2026-07-27 | `docker exec -d autospare_backend python3 /app/maintenance/categorize_parts_batch.py` — processing 865K 'general' + 663K 'service-general' + 142K 'accessories'. Uses FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED so it doesn't block the harvester. Early progress (first 30s): 913 parts got real categories, 4,565 moved to 'כללי'. |
| **New env vars to add to docker-compose.yml** | ⚠️ action needed | Three new env toggles — currently defaulting to 0 (disabled) from within code. To persist across restarts, add to `docker-compose.yml` backend env: `CLEANUP_LLM_ENABLED=0`, `OEM_LOOKUP_ENABLED=0`, `ENRICH_PARTS_ENABLED=0`. Set to 1 only when providers have spare capacity. |

Google notes settings can take 5 min–few hours to propagate. Durable (proper client + baked in .env). |

## Session — 2026-07-25 (price-sync lock + DB task-timeout root-fixes + owner console)

| Item | Status | Detail |
|------|--------|--------|
| **`sync_prices` stuck "dead"/"skipped forever"** | ✅ 2026-07-25 root-fixed + verified | Two bugs. **(1) Lock never freed:** `sync_prices` acquires a Redis lock (4h TTL) but only calls `release()` on its own success path — an error mid-run, or the process being killed (a backend restart/OOM), leaked `autospare:lock:sync_prices` for the full 4h, so every later run logged "skipped — already running on another worker". Fix in `_price_sync_loop` (the sole scheduled runner): clear a lock present at startup (a fresh container's lock is always a killed-run leftover) + force-free it in the run's `finally`. Verified live: startup logged "cleared a stale sync_prices lock", lock now `held=False`. **(2) Run logged "dead" though the work succeeded:** eBay+AliExpress ran ~1.5h on the SHARED `db` session, whose connection was dead by the post-sync steps → "Can't reconnect until invalid transaction is rolled back". Fix: run each provider sync on its OWN fresh session (both commit internally, so it's safe) and `db.rollback()` afterward so the reconciliation/log writes get a live connection. |
| **`task_heal_importer_price` — statement-timeout every cycle (scan-for-nothing)** | ✅ 2026-07-25 fixed | The IL-price backlog was healed to 0 (2026-07-18), so most cycles this task scans 4.2M `parts_catalog` rows for zero matches and — under harvester load — hits the statement timeout (logged ERROR every 30s). Applied the established scan-for-nothing guard (same as `task_recover_priced_inactive`): exponential backoff (30s→30min) when nothing to heal OR on timeout, reset to eager the moment work appears, plus `SET LOCAL statement_timeout='15s'` so a slow scan aborts fast instead of holding a snapshot. |
| **NOA social capability audit (owner: "verify NOA can read activity + reply to DMs/groups/posts")** | ✅ 2026-07-25 audited — NOT implemented | Verified against the code: `social/*_publisher.py` are **publish-only** (`publish`/`is_configured`), with **no** read/comment/DM/mention/reply functions, and there are **no inbound webhooks** for Facebook/Instagram/X/Reddit/Discord comments or DMs (only Telegram, for publishing). So NOA can POST but cannot READ engagement or REPLY to DMs/comments/groups. Building it is a real project: per-platform read+reply APIs, inbound webhooks/polling, and additional platform permissions (Meta messaging scopes need app review) the owner must grant — not a config toggle. Reported with a phased path. |
| **Heavy maintenance tasks time out (`refresh_min_max_prices` 900s, `enrich_pending_parts` 1800s)** | ⚠️ noted, not changed | These hit their hard per-task timeout and log `status=error` some cycles (they often succeed on the next). Left untouched this session to avoid destabilizing the maintenance pipeline; a proper fix is a soft time-budget (stop early with `status=ok, stopped_early=True`) like `lookup_oem_spec`. External/expected noise also present and NOT root-fixable here: eBay 403 cooldowns, HF CLIP 400s, Cerebras/Gemini 429s under load, Playwright page-navigation races. |

## Session — 2026-07-23c (amayama merge check + postgres import optimization + worker scale-up)

| Item | Status | Detail |
|------|--------|--------|
| **Verify migrating the amayama harvester to the HTTP+cookie approach — NOT viable** | ✅ 2026-07-23 tested + verdict | Owner asked to merge amayama onto the same leak-free HTTP mechanism the car-parts harvester now uses. **Tested rigorously** (mint a cf_clearance cookie via a warmed FlareSolverr session + inject the account login cookies, then fetch `/en/search?q=<oem>` via plain `urllib`): for **3 real amayama-priced OEMs**, urllib returned a **reCAPTCHA challenge page** every time (3,481 bytes, `onRecaptchaLoaded`, 0 part rows), while the FlareSolverr browser path is what the live harvester uses. **Verdict: the merge would BREAK amayama** — unlike car-parts.ie (server-rendered, no bot-wall), amayama gates search behind reCAPTCHA that only a real browser passes. amayama MUST stay on FlareSolverr. (Its own slow session-recycle leak is a separate, non-urgent item; not touched, since its authenticated session is delicate and it's currently stable.) |
| **Root-fix + optimize the postgres import path (owner: so we can add more workers / go faster)** | ✅ 2026-07-23 verified | `car_parts_ie_import_generic.py` wrote ~4 INSERTs/part in **autocommit** → ~1,600 **fsync'd commits per 400-part model**, which pinned `postgres_catalog` at ~176% CPU and was the ceiling on harvester workers. The harvest import is **idempotent + re-runnable** (ON CONFLICT; every model re-harvested on a later cycle), so per-row durable fsync is unnecessary. Fix: `SET synchronous_commit = off` (+ `work_mem = 32MB` for the ON CONFLICT index probes) on the import connection — commits stop waiting for disk; a crash loses <1s of writes that the next cycle re-imports (no integrity risk). Autocommit kept, so **locks still release per-row → no added contention** with the harvester/cleanup agents. **Verified live: postgres_catalog ~176% → ~92%, host load ~15 → ~10-11, no failures.** Imports are one-subprocess-per-model (flock-serialized), so faster imports drain the queue faster → more workers now pay off. |
| **Scale car-parts workers 3 → 5** | ✅ 2026-07-23 verified | With ~0.8 postgres cores freed, raised `HARVESTER_PARALLEL_SESSIONS` 3→5 (cheap now — HTTP fetch threads, no browser). **Verified: all 5 workers producing, postgres_catalog ~130% avg, host load ~12 (5-min), 0 failures** — i.e. 5 workers now run COOLER than 3 workers did before the import fix (which sat at 176% postgres / load 15). Net: ~67% more harvest throughput at lower load. Further scaling is bounded by the amayama harvester (~200% on FlareSolverr, unchangeable — needs its browser) and `postgres_catalog`; 5 is the calibrated value, env-tunable via `HARVESTER_PARALLEL_SESSIONS`. |

## Session — 2026-07-23b (harvest load root-fix + Owner WhatsApp console)

Owner: (1) is the 729-empty-slug issue fixed? (2) is the high FlareSolverr CPU / resource use fixed? (3) why did agent/worker failure alerts start — seems tied to the high CPU? Then: connect NOA + AVI to my WhatsApp so I can converse with them, give them tasks, and act on their notifications.

| Item | Status | Detail |
|------|--------|--------|
| **Q1 — 729 wrong-slug 'empty' rows** | ✅ 2026-07-23 fixed | The authoritative slugs are now queued (verified `bmw/x6-e71-e72` present as `car_parts_ie_full/pending`; BMW has all 133 real model slugs), so those brands ARE covered. Also **deleted the 727 stale `empty` `il_market` rows** (guessed from Hebrew names, e.g. `bmw/x6x-drive-30d`) — they were harmless (never reclaimed) but cluttered the picture. Coverage now comes from the authoritative brand-landing slugs. |
| **Q2/Q3 — high CPU + agent/worker failures (ROOT CAUSE: FlareSolverr Chrome leak, not the session count)** | ✅ 2026-07-23 root-fixed + verified | The two are the same incident. Symptom: load avg **18.7** on 6 vCPUs (oversubscribed; normal 8-12), `flaresolverr` at **577% CPU**, and REAL failures — `task_heal_importer_price` + `meili_parity` hitting **statement timeouts** and `sync_prices` marked **dead** (heartbeat CPU-starved) → the owner's failure alert. First lever (harvester `PARALLEL_SESSIONS` 4→2) did NOT drop load, so I stopped guessing and read `top`: **49 chromium processes in the flaresolverr container for only 4 tracked sessions** — a Chrome-process LEAK (FlareSolverr has no session TTL; my ~5 backend restarts during this session's debugging each orphaned Chrome instances that were never reaped). **Root fix:** restarted the FlareSolverr containers → **49→8 chromium, 577%→125% CPU**. Layered fixes so it stays healthy during the multi-week full-catalogue harvest: (a) `PARALLEL_SESSIONS` 4→**2** (env `HARVESTER_PARALLEL_SESSIONS`, in docker-compose) — 4 was fine when the IL queue drained fast and the harvester idled; with the 6,000-model backlog it runs flat-out. (b) `INTER_MODEL` 5→**20 s** (env `HARVESTER_INTER_MODEL_S`) — a real gap between models gives duty-cycle headroom so CF solves don't starve the DB tasks. **Verified: 0 statement-timeout / dead-worker errors in the 10 min after the fix** (was firing every cycle). **The leak is ONGOING, not just a debugging artifact** — chrome climbed back 8→40 in ~35 min under 2 sessions. First mitigation was a reaper cron, but the owner (correctly) rejected "restart the container every 15 min" as a band-aid and asked for the actual leak fixed. **TRUE ROOT CAUSE (found 2026-07-23):** the leaked chromium are **live processes reparented to PID 1 (state `S`/`R`, NOT zombies)** — dumb-init reaping is fine — and FlareSolverr **orphans the Chrome process on `sessions.destroy`** instead of killing it (verified: destroying all sessions reaped only 40→30). The deeper problem: the harvester routed **every page** (~80/model, endlessly) through FlareSolverr's browser — a browser has no business being your HTTP client. A persistent-session-pool attempt only *bounded* it (chrome plateaued ~40, still 1 failure). **REAL ROOT FIX — FlareSolverr is no longer the fetch engine:** car-parts.ie is server-rendered (the full-catalogue seeder already fetches it with plain `urllib`+cookie), so the harvester now uses FlareSolverr **ONLY to mint a `cf_clearance` cookie (~2×/hour; the solve session is destroyed immediately)** and fetches **every page via plain `urllib` + that cookie** (`http_get`; re-mints on a 403/503). No persistent browser, no per-page browser call → orphaned-Chrome accumulation is structurally impossible. Removed the session pool / per-cycle create-destroy / worker session param; workers are now cheap HTTP threads. **VERIFIED LIVE:** `cf_clearance minted via flaresolverr2 (13 cookies)` → harvested `alfa-romeo/spider-105 → 395 parts`, `spider-115 → 388 parts` via HTTP (full parts, no browser); **FS chrome dropped to ~9 and held** (vs 40-52 climbing), **host load 21→10**. The reaper cron was **removed** (`deploy/flaresolverr_reaper.sh` kept only as a manual tool; it should now never fire). **Honest caveat given to owner:** the 6-vCPU box IS the upgrade (4→6 vCPU, active — corrected: it was NOT a pending change) — the box still reports **6 vCPU / 11 GB** (Contabo resizes need a stop/start from the panel); the fixes are tuned for the real current capacity and are env-tunable up once more cores are live. **Lesson: when a CPU number won't drop after the obvious lever, read `top` for leaked/zombie processes before tuning further — and rapid successive container restarts leak FlareSolverr Chrome (restart the FS container after a heavy restart cycle).** |
| **Owner WhatsApp Console — converse with AVI/NOA + act on notifications (owner request)** | ✅ 2026-07-23 built + verified | New `agents/owner_console.py`, wired into `routes/webhooks.py`: when the OWNER's number (`OWNER_WHATSAPP_PHONE` +972586050155) messages the platform WhatsApp, it is intercepted **before** the customer brain and routed to his private console (never treats him as a customer; never self-loops the platform's own number). **Two-way agent chat in OWNER MODE:** default **AVI** (orchestrator), prefix "נועה"/"noa" → **NOA** (social) — each answered by a DIRECT grounded LLM call seeded with a **live system-status block** (harvest coverage, catalog counts, recent worker failures, load) + a short rolling history (Redis, 24 h). *(AVI is normally a JSON routing classifier — calling it for freeform chat produced garbage dashes; fixed by calling `hf_text` directly with an owner system prompt instead of the router agent.)* **Deterministic commands (bilingual HE/EN):** `סטטוס`/status (full live review), `שאיבה`/harvester (harvest progress), `פוסטים`/posts (list pending NOA posts), `אשר <id>`/approve (mark approved + **publish** via `social/registry.dispatch` to each platform), `דחה <id>`/reject, `עזרה`/help. So the owner can now **act on NOA's approval notifications by replying** `אשר`/`דחה`. Uses its own CATALOG-DB session (those tables aren't in the PII DB the webhook passes). **Verified live:** `is_owner` matches owner-only; `status` returned real data (750/6,679 models, 4.15M catalog, flagged `sync_prices dead` + load); `posts` listed the 6 pending NOA posts with short-ids; AVI + NOA gave clean, useful, owner-aware Hebrew replies; the go-live menu was delivered to the owner's WhatsApp (`ok:True`). |
| **Owner console didn't respond to WhatsApp — WhatsApp LID, not phone (owner bug report)** | ✅ 2026-07-23 fixed | The owner sent a NOA command and got no reply. Root cause: WhatsApp now routes his device by a **stable LID** (`98058566160397@lid`), not his phone number — his inbound `from` arrived as the LID, so `is_owner()` (phone-only) returned False and the message **fell through to the customer bot** (confirmed: his 17:41 conversation was stored under `whatsapp_phone = 98058566160397@lid`). Fix: `is_owner` now matches the owner by phone **OR** LID (`OWNER_WHATSAPP_LID`, set in docker-compose; the bridge already forwards `@lid` JIDs). **Verified:** `is_owner('whatsapp:98058566160397@lid')` → True, phone still True, random customer → False. |

Owner goal (`/goal`): "fix noa language and posts — I keep getting robotic posts and campaigns; send NOA's posts and notifications to WhatsApp instead of Telegram; posts should be smart, human, funny and attractive, and be SELLING posts; add a QR code instead of the platform links so the scanner picks the platform he likes; add more automotive hashtags (also Arabic); notifications must not be sent at 3:00 AM; the cart 3-reminder rule keeps being broken; NOA should write like a human; and backend issues must reach my WhatsApp, not sit silently in the DB."

| Item | Status | Detail |
|------|--------|--------|
| **OCR ad-filter blinded by a source DOWNGRADE — branded/disclaimer images leaked into the bucket** | ✅ 2026-07-20 fixed + quantified | Owner spotted a served thumbnail carrying a "UMPA" brand logo and "ACTUAL PRODUCT MAY VARY / Product image for illustration purposes only" — which the owner rule (no label, no brand name) should have rejected. The filter was not broken and tesseract was healthy (5.5.0): `_is_promo` OCR'd the image and genuinely read **0 words**. Root cause is one line in `_best_source`: `re.sub(r"/s-l\d{2,4}\.", "/s-l500.", u)` rewrites **any** eBay size token to `s-l500`. It was written to UPGRADE tiny thumbs (`s-l225`→`s-l500`) but equally **DOWNGRADES** large ones (`s-l1600`→`s-l500`) — and at 500px the disclaimer text is too small for tesseract to read. Measured on the exact leaked image: `s-l500` → **0 words → accepted**; `s-l1600` (1400×1400) → **12 words** ("ACTUAL","PRODUCT","MAY","VARY","Product","image","for","illustration") → **rejected**. So the line meant to improve source quality is precisely what blinded the ad-filter. Fix: request the LARGEST variant (`s-l1600`) — better OCR **and** a crisper source for the 500×500 thumbnail, matching the intent of the autoteile-meile `m=0` rule directly below it — plus a new `_fetch_source()` that falls back to the smaller variant if the CDN lacks that size, so no part loses its image. Verified: the leaked image is now `_is_promo → True` (rejected). **Blast radius quantified rather than assumed:** a random 40-thumbnail sample re-checked at full resolution shows **1/40 (~2.5%)** would now be rejected ⇒ roughly **640 of 25,451** existing thumbnails are contaminated; the rest are genuinely clean. Those predate the fix and need a re-audit pass (deferred — the box was at load ~21 and a 25K re-fetch is heavy). **Lesson: a content filter is only as good as the input it is given — "upgrade" logic that can silently downgrade turns a working filter into a no-op.** |
| **Arabic search returned ZERO results — no Arabic in the catalog and no Arabic query bridge** | ✅ 2026-07-20 verified | Owner asked to verify Arabic support. Live test: `فلتر زيت`, `فحمات فرامل`, `بطارية` → **0 results each**. Root cause: the catalog is English + Hebrew — exactly **1** active part contains any Arabic text — and while Hebrew queries are bridged to English by `expand_hebrew_query` (33 terms) **there was no Arabic equivalent**, so Arabic tokens matched no document. Fix: `_AR_EXPANSIONS` (~120 automotive terms + brands/models) and `expand_arabic_query` in `hf_client.py`, plus `_normalize_arabic` folding the orthographic variants customers actually type (أ إ آ ٱ→ا, ة→ه, ى→ي, harakat stripped); longest-key-first so `فلتر زيت`→`oil filter`, not `filter`+`oil`. New single entry point `expand_query` (Hebrew AND Arabic) is what `routes/parts.py` search now calls. **Critical detail:** Arabic expansion returns **English-ONLY** on a hit (non-Arabic tokens such as a model or year are preserved). Keeping the Arabic words in — as the Hebrew path correctly does, since `name_he` really exists — left Meilisearch searching for tokens no document contains and still returned 0; that intermediate version was tested and rejected. **Verified live through the public domain:** `فلتر زيت` → **30 results**, `فحمات فرامل` → **48**, `بطارية` → **29**. |
| **Amayama harvester silent 6.7 days — NOT the login cookie** | ✅ 2026-07-20 diagnosed + restored | Presented as a dead harvester; the documented playbook blames the expired `ama_ssid_s` cookie. Tested the **stored** cookie server-side through FlareSolverr first: the page came back with `Log Out` + the Balance widget — **the session was still valid**, so replacing it would have been wasted work on a wrong diagnosis. Real cause: **FlareSolverr contention.** `fs_request` waits up to `maxTimeout/1000+20` = **140 s** per call, and FlareSolverr was pinned at ~374 % CPU serving the car-parts.ie sessions, so Amayama's 40-OEM rounds each queued behind them — a round takes ~1.5 h and only logs **at the end**, so a working harvester looks hung. Confirmed alive by sampling `/proc/<pid>` CPU ticks (climbing) with 1 open socket. Killed the process so the supervisor relaunched it (rc=-15, relaunched in 60 s, now progressing). **Lesson: a harvester that logs once per round is indistinguishable from a hung one — diagnose from CPU/socket state, not log age, and prove the credential is bad before replacing it.** |

| **Owner WhatsApp console: AVI answered when talking to NOA (no session)** | ✅ 2026-08-04 fixed + verified | Owner: "when I call NOA and start talking to her, AVI answers." Cause: `_pick_agent` routed EVERY message independently — `@נועה <msg>` reached NOA for that one message, but any follow-up WITHOUT the `@` prefix fell to the `else` branch → `router_agent` (AVI). No conversation state. Fix: a **sticky agent session** in Redis (`owner:console:active_agent`, 60-min idle TTL, env `OWNER_ACTIVE_AGENT_TTL_S`). An explicit `@agent` switches AND pins; an unprefixed message continues with the *active* agent; `יציאה`/`תפריט` clears it back to neutral (default AVI). Each reply stays signed `[NOA]/[AVI]/[עוזר]`, and an explicit switch shows "🔀 עכשיו בשיחה עם X". Verified by simulating the exact reported flow: `@נועה …` → NOA, unprefixed follow-up → **still NOA** (was AVI), `@אבי …` → switches, `יציאה` → back to default. |
| **Owner notifications: same template re-sent ~100×/day with no real change** | ✅ 2026-08-04 fixed | Owner: "notifications isn't updated, it's a template that keeps sending without a real update." Measured in the DB: the SAME 5 notifications sent **310 times each over 3 days** — "⚠️ תשלום לספק נכשל עבור <order>" (×4 orders) and "🤖 5 הזמנות תקועות טופלו אוטומטית". Cause: `_stuck_orders_monitor_loop` re-triggers `trigger_supplier_fulfillment` on the SAME 5 orders every 30 min (their supplier payment keeps failing — genuinely stuck), and BOTH the monitor's "N stuck orders handled" notification AND the "supplier payment failed" notification inside `trigger_supplier_fulfillment` fired to every admin EACH cycle, with zero dedup. Root fix — **notify by exception** on both (the same lesson already applied to the status loop + harvest supervisor): the stuck-set notification only fires when the sorted order-number SET changes (Redis signature, 24h TTL), and the supplier-payment-failed notification dedups per (order, failure_reason) with a 24h cooldown. A NEW failure or changed set still alerts immediately; a persistent one re-alerts at most once/day. **The retry itself is unchanged** (insufficient_funds orders are meant to retry until funds arrive) — only the notification spam is fixed. |
| **QR post image showed REVERSED Hebrew (word-salad)** | ✅ 2026-07-20 fixed + verified | Owner screenshotted the composed QR image (`/thumbnails/thumbs/qr/…jpg`) — its Hebrew read "יקלח ףוליח" for "חלקי חילוף", i.e. every word character-reversed. Cause: `social/qr_media.py` rendered text with PIL's BASIC layout engine and a manual `_shape_hebrew()` that reversed each word — the classic wrong way to do RTL in an image. This Pillow (12.3.0) is built WITH libraqm (`features.check('raqm')=True`), which implements the Unicode bidi algorithm + HarfBuzz shaping. Fix: load fonts with `layout_engine=ImageFont.Layout.RAQM` and pass **logical-order** Hebrew (no manual reversal; `_shape_hebrew` is now identity). Verified by rendering the composite and READING the JPEG back (both directly and fetched through the public domain): "חלקי חילוף לרכב — לפי מספר רישוי" and "סרקו את הקוד — ובחרו איפה נוח לכם לדבר איתנו" now render correctly right-to-left; QR unchanged (still decodes to the hub URL). **Same lesson as the 2026-07-28/29 visual-order incidents: a reversal-looking RTL defect in a RENDERER is a bidi problem — never reverse the stored/logical string; use a bidi-aware layout.** |
| **NOA semantic guard (local MiniLM) threshold rejected LEGITIMATE posts** | ✅ 2026-07-20 recalibrated + verified | Owner asked to verify NOA's connection to the local MiniLM embedding model. It IS wired: `_noa_enqueue_social_post` → `social/post_guard.check` → `category_embed` (local `paraphrase-multilingual-MiniLM-L12-v2`, ONNX, he/ar/en) → cosine similarity between the caption and the part it advertises, to catch grounding failures (the model once sold WIPER-blade copy as a SUNSHADE). Verifying it surfaced a real bug: `NOA_GUARD_MIN_SIM=0.40` was calibrated on n=12 short synthetic pairs and **rejects real marketing captions**. Measured on real posts: correct-part captions score **0.227–0.483** (a long emoji/price/CTA caption vs a short part label is inherently low-cosine), while the gross grounding failures the guard exists to catch score **0.076–0.099**. At 0.40, **3 of 4 legitimate posts were rejected** → the guard silently suppressed good NOA posts (it returns None → not queued). No clean cut exists for SUBTLE same-domain mixups (battery-vs-wiper scored 0.384), and the guard is explicitly fail-OPEN, so the right threshold catches only the gross class. Lowered to **0.15** (below every legitimate post, above the sunshade band). Verified live: 4/4 real posts PASS, sunshade-as-brake-pads REJECT. **Lesson: a quality gate calibrated on synthetic data must be re-measured against REAL output before it goes live — a mis-set threshold that fails-closed silently deletes the very thing it guards.** |
| **🔴 ROOT CAUSE (4th report) — agents sound robotic / "wrong sentence build": temperature was NEVER sent to the model** | ✅ 2026-07-20 root-fixed + verified | The owner reported robotic NOA posts for the FOURTH time; the previous three passes all tweaked NOA's sanitizers, which was the wrong layer. This time I captured the RAW model output before any post-processing and it was garbled at the SOURCE: `הקפאתם את הקופה?` (meant coffee קפה, wrote cash-register קופה), `מכבסים את הקופה` (does-laundry-on-the-cash-register), `מצבת הרוח` (tombstone, meant mood מצב הרוח), `יכולה לחזור את ה-Corolla` (broken verb). Root cause: **`hf_text`/`hf_text_fast`/`_cerebras_call` never sent a `temperature`, so every agent ran at Cerebras's server default (~1.0)**, and `gpt-oss-120b` at 1.0 writes creative-but-WRONG Hebrew words. Every agent defines a `temperature` attribute (router 0.1 … NOA 0.9) but **`.think()` never passed it** — all of it was dead code. Proven with a live A/B on the identical prompt: temp 1.0 → "משמש כקול פנקס" (garble); temp 0.4/0.2 → clean, human, idiomatic Hebrew. **Root fix (whole model layer, not NOA):** threaded `temperature` through `hf_text`→`_cerebras_call`→payload (and into the cache key so a 0.35 result can't be served for a 0.9 request); `.think()` now sends `min(self.temperature, 0.6)` for EVERY agent; NOA lowered 0.9→**0.35** (`NOA_GEN_TEMPERATURE`), and the marketing-loop + `generate_post` direct calls pass it. **Two secondary sentence-manglers fixed in the same pass:** (1) the control-char stripper in `_normalize_noa_symbols` removed U+200D (ZWJ), splitting compound emoji (👩‍💻→👩💻) — now preserves ZWJ + U+FE0F; (2) `_is_low_quality_caption`'s lone-Hebrew-letter garble check flagged the trailing מ of **מע"מ** (VAT — in every priced post) and the prefix מ of **מ-₪43**, because the gershayim quote / hyphen read as a word boundary; that sent every priced post to the repair path which stapled "אנחנו מוכרים חלקי חילוף בלבד" onto the last sentence. Rewrote the check to flag only a **space-isolated** lone letter, killing the whole false-positive class (prefixes, abbreviations, price forms) while still catching genuine floating-letter garble. **Verified live end-to-end:** two fresh generations (Corolla/oil-filter, Sportage/brake-pads) produce clean, human, witty Hebrew — real price intact, emoji intact, structure intact, NO stapled boilerplate, trilingual hashtags. **Lesson: when output quality is wrong, read the RAW model output FIRST — three sessions "fixed" the sanitizers because nobody checked whether the model itself was the problem. It was.** |
| **🔴 CRITICAL — site search returned ZERO results for every query (case-sensitive part_type)** | ✅ 2026-07-20 verified | Found while verifying search on the owner's request. `/api/v1/parts/search` returned empty buckets for `brake pads`, `oil filter` and `רפידות בלם` — **the core product function was down**, and silently: HTTP 200, no error logged. Meilisearch was healthy (4,202,838 docs, returning hits) and the DB had matching parts with available, priced, active suppliers — results were being discarded *after* retrieval. Root cause: `_fetch_three_buckets` calls `_fetch_type(["Original"])` / `(["OEM"])` / `(["Aftermarket","Refurbished"])` — the **canonical capitalized** vocabulary from `PART_TYPE_MAP` — but the live catalog stores the **raw lowercase** forms (`oem` 3,110,111 · `aftermarket` 878,529 · `oe_equivalent` 137,939), because `normalize_part_types` never converted those 4.1M rows. The predicate `pc.part_type = ANY(:pt)` is **case-SENSITIVE**, so every bucket matched 0 rows. Proven with one query varying only case: `ANY(['OEM'])` → **0**, `ANY(['oem'])` → **364**; `ANY(['Aftermarket'])` → **0**, `ANY(['aftermarket'])` → **15**. Fix (both the Meili and ILIKE code paths): compare `lower(btrim(pc.part_type))` against **lowercase synonym lists** (`_PT_ORIGINAL`/`_PT_OEM`/`_PT_AFTERMARKET`) mirroring `PART_TYPE_MAP`, so search works whether a row holds `oem` or `OEM` — it cannot re-break when the normalizer later capitalizes the column, and it needs no 4.1M-row migration to restore service. **Verified live through the public domain:** `brake pads` → 30 results, `oil filter` → 48, `רפידות בלם` → 48, each with priced suppliers. **This is the same class of bug as the `'New'`→`'new'` and Hebrew-vs-English-category defects already in the mistake log: a hardcoded vocabulary drifting from what the DB actually stores.** |
| **Robotic posts — ROOT CAUSE #1: every post quoting a REAL PRICE was flattened + boiler-plated** | ✅ 2026-07-20 verified | The prior session fixed the sanitizers that flattened line breaks, but a deeper trigger survived: `_is_low_quality_caption`'s garble detector `\b[א-ת]\b` (a lone Hebrew letter) **matched the ordinary Hebrew price construct `מ-198`** ("from ₪198") — and `ב-2020`, `ב AutoSpareFinder`, `ה Corolla`, `ל Toyota`. Any post containing those was judged low-quality and sent to `_repair_low_quality_caption`, which (a) collapsed `\s+`→" ", **destroying every line break**, and (b) stapled the canned sentence "אנחנו מוכרים חלקי חילוף בלבד." So **precisely the selling posts with real catalog prices — the ones the owner wants most — were the ones turned into robotic run-on paragraphs.** Root fix: the lone-letter rule now exempts the Hebrew one-letter prefixes (מ/ב/ל/ה/ו/ש/כ/ד) when bound by hyphen or space to a number or Latin token, while **genuine garble is still caught** (verified: `א ב ג ד רפידות…` → still flagged); and `_repair_low_quality_caption` now preserves line structure (collapses intra-line whitespace only), matching the rule already applied to `_enforce_sales_only`/`_enforce_tiktok_ads_policy`. **Verified:** 5-line post with `מ-198` → `low_quality=False`, 7 lines preserved, price hyphen intact, zero stapled boilerplate. |

| **Hashtags — Arabic support added (owner: "hashtags also in arabic")** | ✅ 2026-07-20 verified | `_NOA_HASHTAG_RE` only accepted `[A-Za-z0-9_֐-׿]`, so **every Arabic hashtag was silently dropped** by `_filter_hashtags`. Added the Arabic range `؀-ۿ` to the tag regex, introduced `_NOA_RTL_TAG_RE` (Hebrew **or** Arabic passes as-is; Latin still allowlist-gated), and added Arabic (`#قطع_غيار`, `#صيانة_سيارات`, `#ميكانيكي`, `#عالم_السيارات`, …) + expanded Hebrew and English automotive pools. **Also fixed a silent mangler:** `_normalize_noa_symbols` stripped `_` as markdown emphasis, turning `#قطع_غيار` into the unsearchable `#قطعغيار`; hashtags are now protected during that substitution. **Verified live:** posts carry 9 tags spanning Hebrew + Arabic + English with underscores intact. |
| **QR funnel replaces platform links (owner directive)** | ✅ 2026-07-20 verified | New `social/qr_media.py` + `routes/connect.py`. Every NOA post's media is now a **1080×1080 composite**: the part thumbnail (or a branded canvas when no clean thumbnail matches) + a white QR badge + a Hebrew "scan me" strip, compressed to JPEG and uploaded **content-addressed** (`thumbs/qr/<sha256>.jpg`) to the existing private thumbnail bucket, served via the existing `/api/v1/thumbnails/` proxy (the `thumbs/` prefix was already allowlisted, and Cloudflare edge-caches it). The QR encodes `GET /api/v1/go?src=qr_<platform>_w<week>` → a self-contained tri-lingual (HE/AR/EN) RTL picker page where the visitor chooses WhatsApp / Telegram / Website / Facebook / Instagram themselves; `src` is sanitized to `[a-z0-9_-]` and propagated as UTM so QR traffic is measurable per platform/week. The page does **no DB access**, so it cannot become a load vector. Router registered in `BACKEND_API_ROUTES.py`. **Verified end-to-end:** page returns 200 through the public domain; media builds + uploads (59,963 B JPEG, HTTP 200, `image/jpeg`); and the QR **decoded out of the FINAL composed image** (cv2, the real scan scenario) → exactly `https://autosparefinder.co.il/api/v1/go?src=qr_instagram_w30`. `qrcode>=8.0` added to `requirements.txt` and **baked into the image** (rebuilt + recreated — not left as a runtime pip install). |

| **3:00 AM notifications — GLOBAL quiet hours (owner: "it's not possible to send at 3:00 am")** | ✅ 2026-07-20 verified | Only the abandoned-cart loop had a daytime window; **the pending-payment reminder loop, every owner/admin health alert, the supervised-task death alert, the status digest and NOA all sent around the clock** — the 03:00 messages. Added a single enforcement point: `_notify_window_open()` + `_wa_send_quiet()` (09:00–21:00 IL, `NOTIFY_SEND_START/END_HOUR_IL`). NOA no longer free-runs on a 24h timer anchored to container start (which drifted into the night after restarts) — it fires at a fixed IL local time (`NOA_POST_HOUR_IL`, default 09:30) and its weekday/season logic now uses IL local time, not UTC. **Verified:** 03:00/06:00/08:00/21:00/23:00 → blocked; 09:00/13:00/20:00 → send; a simulated night alert queued (not sent) and was recoverable from Redis (test entry cleaned up, no message delivered to the owner). |
| **Cart reminder cap kept being broken — ROOT CAUSE: rolling window, not a cap** | ✅ 2026-07-20 verified live | `ABANDONED_CART_MAX_SENDS_PER_WINDOW=3` counted reminders only **within a rolling 3-day window**, so every 3 days the same cart legitimately earned 3 MORE sends — forever. The live DB proved it: one cart had received **48 reminders**. Fixed to a **lifetime cap per cart** (no time cutoff) plus a **minimum 24 h gap** between consecutive reminders (`ABANDONED_CART_MIN_GAP_H`). Applied the same fix to the pending-payment loop, which had **no cap at all** — only a 6 h dedup, allowing ~4 reminders/day indefinitely; now capped at `PAYMENT_REMINDER_MAX_SENDS=3` per order lifetime. **Verified live in logs:** `Skip cart ba23170a… — lifetime cap reached (48/3 reminders ever sent)`. |
| **Backend errors sat silently in the DB (owner: "notify my WhatsApp, don't keep it silent until I check")** | ✅ 2026-07-20 | `run_all_tasks` recorded per-task `status: "error"` into its report/`job_registry` and moved on — no alert unless the whole cycle died, so individual task failures were invisible until someone looked. Added an alert at the end of every cycle: when `tasks_error > 0` it WhatsApps the owner the failing task names + truncated errors, **deduplicated by a hash of the failing-task SET with a 24 h TTL** — so a persistent failure doesn't nag every 3 h, but a *new* failure alerts immediately. Sent through `_wa_send_quiet` (queued at night, delivered in the morning) with a direct-send fallback if the import fails. |
| **NOA personality — smart / funny / human / selling** | ✅ 2026-07-20 verified | Rewrote the system-prompt writing block into an explicit personality contract: a real, witty person who knows cars (not a marketing department); **smart** (teach one small true thing per post), **funny** (exactly one clever wink — no forced jokes), **human** (first person, short sentences, spoken Hebrew, ≤3 emoji), **selling** (every post is pain → solution → real price → ONE clear CTA; no empty brand-awareness posts), **engaging** (close with an easy, fun question). Added a prohibition on writing its own disclosure/legal sentences inline (the system appends them on their own line only when needed) — the model had been echoing "אנחנו מוכרים חלקי חילוף בלבד" into the body. Mirrored the same instructions into the daily-post and Monday-campaign prompts in `_noa_marketing_loop`. **Verified with two LIVE generations** (Instagram/Corolla wheel bearings, TikTok/Sportage brake pads): human story-telling opener, a mechanic's tip, the real catalog price, a single plate-search CTA, an engagement question, multi-line structure preserved, trilingual hashtags, no link-footer spam. |
| **NOA skills documented + wired** | ✅ 2026-07-20 | `docs/skills.md` NOA section rewritten from 3 stale Telegram-era skills to the real, wired capability set: `CONTENT_GENERATE` (catalog-grounded), `HUMAN_VOICE_WRITE`, `QR_FUNNEL`, `HASHTAG_MIX`, `WHATSAPP_APPROVAL`, `CAMPAIGN_BRIEF`, `PUBLISH` — each naming the module/function that implements it and the schedule it runs on. Covers: **auth** (no/invalid/revoked key → 401; `/health` open; every data endpoint requires a key), **data-leakage** (asserts NO `supplier`/`cost`/`importer_price`/`online_price`/`base_price`/`min|max_price`/`margin`/`profit`/internal flags anywhere in any response, and ONLY whitelisted keys), **rate limiting** (rate=3 key → `[200,200,200,429,429,429]`), **injection** (SQLi payloads in `q`/`manufacturer` → parameterized-safe, no SQL error/stack leaked), **input validation** (`limit>50`→422, negative offset→422, missing q+manufacturer→400), **error hygiene** (bad id → clean 404, body has no traceback/paths/SQL), **method** (POST→405), **internal endpoints still protected** (admin/auth/system → 401/403/405), **key storage** (sha256 only, 0 plaintext). **3 issues found and root-fixed during the test:** (1) non-uuid `part_id` → **500→404** (validate UUIDs before the DB cast); (2) manufacturer browse `ORDER BY name` over 90K-part brands = **40s+ sort / DoS → 0.9s** (pick a page of ids first, then price — no full-brand sort); (3) a bogus manufacturer made `LIMIT` walk the whole 4.1M-row table (**DoS**) → now validated against `car_brands` first, unknown brand returns empty with no scan. **Result: 32/32 PASS.** Re-run any time: `python3 /app/devtests/api_security_test.py`. Test keys cleaned up (0 keys in DB until a real partner is onboarded). |
| **Importers left `importer_price_ils = 0` with margin-less `base_price` — ROOT FIX (owner catch)** | ✅ 2026-07-18 verified | Owner: "the importer should bring prices, not 0." Found **3,627 IL-sourced parts** (mostly AutoParts Pro IL, the platform's primary IL supplier) with `importer_price_ils = 0` AND `base_price = cost` (the **45% margin was missing** — e.g. cost 100 stored as base 100 instead of 145). Root cause: `task_heal_importer_price` (db_cleanup_agent) had an over-restrictive guard `AND (base_price = 0)` that **permanently skipped any part whose base_price was already written** but whose importer cost was left 0 → a stuck backlog. **Fix:** rewrote the heal task to (a) drop the `base_price=0` guard, (b) add `online_price_ils=0` so it only heals IL-sourced parts (never touches foreign parts, whose cost is `online_price_ils` and `importer=0` is correct), (c) set `importer_price_ils` = the cheapest **IL supplier's recorded ex-VAT cost** (same value the customer price derives from → fields stay consistent, customer prices don't move) and `base_price = cost×1.45`, (d) `FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED` so it never fights the harvester for row locks. Backfilled the 3,627 → **gap now 0**. **VAT policy verified end-to-end (owner reminder "IL importers have 18% VAT"):** `get_supplier_vat_rate` → IL/Israel **0.18**, foreign (IE/US) **0.0**; a real healed IL part prices cost 100 → base 145 (×1.45) → **VAT 26.10 (18%)** → total **₪171.10**; foreign 100 → 145 → VAT 0 → 145. `base = cost×1.45` holds on every surface. |

## Session — 2026-07-13 (Alert-noise root-fixes + cart pay-link + NOA language + brand logos)

Owner report: still getting "anomaly" + "dead worker" WhatsApp alerts; NOA posts read robotic/incoherent; the cart-notification payment link still isn't pressable; consider brand logos instead of names.

| Item | Status | Detail |
|------|--------|--------|
| **"⚠️ Watchdog anomaly detected" false alerts — ROOT FIX** | ✅ 2026-07-13 verified | The stall-watchdog kills a genuinely-stuck **same-container** DB query (legitimate) but recorded it under action `kill_orphan` with details `"zombie: same-container…"`. `validate_watchdog_actions` only accepts `kill_orphan` when details contain `"predates"` → **every legitimate zombie kill was flagged an anomaly** and WhatsApp'd. Confirmed one fired today (killed pid 2457488 after 2743s). Fix: gave the zombie kill its own action **`kill_zombie`** (BACKEND_API_ROUTES.py), validator now judges it on its own terms (dur_s ≥ 2400s = legit, not an anomaly) + counts it in the burst tally (db_update_agent.py); added `kill_zombie` to `watchdog_state.py` (Literal + stats). **Verified:** simulated a 2743s `kill_zombie` event → 0 anomalies. |
| **"🔴 Worker failed" / "Zombie auto-killed" false alerts on restart — ROOT FIX** | ✅ 2026-07-13 verified live | Every backend restart (deploy/OOM/SIGKILL — 15+ today) orphans in-flight jobs; a later sweep flips them `running→failed`, and the HealthMonitor/status-loop then alert the owner though nothing actually failed. `_reconcile_orphaned_jobs` catches most at startup, but the ungraceful-kill window still leaked alerts. Added a **container-lifetime guard** to all three alert senders: HealthMonitor **5a** (failed/dead) and **5b** (zombie sweep) now require `COALESCE(last_heartbeat_at, started_at) >= _BACKEND_START_UTC`, and the 30-min status loop's `failed_jobs` filter applies the same rule — a job whose last activity predates the current container is a restart-orphan (silent), a job that failed *within* this container still alerts. **Verified live:** a `sync_prices` row sitting in `dead` (restart-orphan) is now correctly **SUPPRESSED**, not alerted. |
| **Cart-notification payment link — bare API path → pressable /pay/ link** | ✅ 2026-07-13 verified | Both the abandoned-cart AND pending-payment WhatsApp messages literally embedded the string `/api/v1/customers/cart` — not a URL, not tappable, not even a customer page. **Abandoned cart** (`_build_abandoned_cart_whatsapp_message`): now mints a real one-tap canonical `/pay/` Stripe link via `create_checkout_link` for single-item carts (routes through the SAME server-side pricing as Stripe/website — never the raw cart `unit_price`, which is supplier COST), full clickable cart URL for multi-item. **Pending payment** (`_build_pending_payment_whatsapp_message`): new `regenerate_order_pay_link()` in routes/payments.py builds a fresh Stripe link from the existing order's already-priced OrderItems (Stripe URLs expire 24h, so regenerate per reminder). Messages now put a full `https://…/pay/XXX` on its own line so WhatsApp linkifies it. **Verified:** all three message builders emit a pressable https link, zero `/api/v1/...` paths; helpers import clean. |
| **NOA posts robotic/incoherent — sanitizer was mangling good copy** | ✅ 2026-07-13 verified | The system prompt is strong; the **post-processing** destroyed coherence: (1) `_enforce_sales_only` AND `_enforce_tiktok_ads_policy` flattened every newline into one run-on paragraph (killed the hook-line/short-body rhythm); (2) blanket word-subs (`מוסך`→`חנות חלקים`, `תיקון`→`התאמת חלק`) turned legit customer-pain copy ("בלי לרוץ בין מוסכים") into broken Hebrew; (3) 2–3 canned boilerplate sentences (`_ensure_platform_value_points` + duplicate "מוכרים חלקי חילוף בלבד") were stapled onto **every** post = template feel; (4) the low-quality gate's 14-word floor force-replaced legitimately-short TikTok posts with a generic fallback. **Fixes:** rewrote `_enforce_sales_only` to preserve line breaks and only rewrite genuine FIRST-PERSON "we repair/install cars" claims (new narrow `_NOA_FIRST_PERSON_SERVICE_RE`), append the parts-only disclosure at most once and only when a claim was actually stripped; made `_enforce_tiktok_ads_policy` preserve lines (per-line compliance rewrites); removed forced value-point stapling from the publish path (kept as advisory in `review_post_policy`); lowered the low-quality floor 14→8. **Verified:** a 3-line Corolla brake post keeps all lines on both TikTok and non-TikTok, no boilerplate stapled, `מוסכים` intact, ≤1 disclosure. |
| **Parts page hangs ~68s on load — manufacturers brand-list query (owner: "pages slow after login")** | ✅ 2026-07-14 verified | Measured all post-login calls: per-page Layout calls are fast (auth/me 0.28s, notifications 0.009s, cart 0.012s) — but `/api/v1/parts/manufacturers` took **~68s**. It powers the brand dropdown the parts/search page fetches on load, so that page hung. Root: `SELECT … CROSS JOIN LATERAL jsonb_array_elements(compatible_vehicles)` unnests millions of JSON arrays across 4.18M rows — no index can fix an unnest+aggregate; the 10-min cache still let every expiry/restart re-eat 68s inline on a user request. Fix: **stale-while-revalidate** (`get_manufacturers` always serves the cached copy instantly; on staleness it refreshes in the BACKGROUND via `_refresh_manufacturers_cache`, never blocking the user), **TTL 10min→6h** (list barely changes), and a **startup pre-warm** (`_warm_search_paths` schedules the refresh as a background task — not awaited, so it doesn't delay readiness by 68s). **Verified: 68s → 0.03s.** |
| **Frontend first-load heavy — one 848KB JS bundle, no code-splitting (owner: "pages slow after login")** | ✅ 2026-07-14 verified | After confirming the per-page API calls are fast, the remaining first-load cost was the SPA shipping as a single `index-*.js` of **848KB** (866,874 bytes) containing every route — so the first page after login downloaded/parsed the whole app (Admin + Parts + Agents + Chat dominated) before rendering. Fix: **route-level code-splitting** in `App.jsx` — every page is now `lazy(() => import('./pages/X'))` behind a `<Suspense>` with a spinner fallback (Login + LandingPage kept eager to avoid a flash on the two commonest first-paints). **Verified after rebuild: 848KB → 44 chunks; main chunk 295KB (−65%);** Admin (173KB) and Parts (145KB) now load ONLY when those pages are visited. index.html references the new main chunk (200), lazy chunks serve on demand (200). |
| **Meilisearch sync "crash" — MeilisearchTimeoutError on the final indexing wait** | ✅ 2026-07-14 | Owner reported "Meili crashed yesterday". The CONTAINER was fine (up 6d, no OOM, last restart 2026-07-07) — it was the **sync** that errored: `MeilisearchTimeoutError: timeout of 900000ms (15 min) exceeded waiting for pending update`. Root: `meili_sync.py` fires every batch without waiting then waits ≤15 min for the LAST task — but Meilisearch processes tasks sequentially, so on a large sync the last task sits behind a huge queue that takes >15 min to drain, and the wait times out and raises, killing the whole sync (even though indexing is fine and continues; checkpoints are saved per-batch). The settings task already handled this non-fatally; the batch wait didn't. Fix: wrap the final `_wait_task` in try/except (timeout 15→30 min) → log non-fatal, let Meili finish in the background, and the post-sync `_meili_verify_parity` catches genuine drift. Index currently healthy: 4.20M docs, isIndexing=false. |
| **Full price-policy audit — search DISPLAY applied flat VAT (client saw ~18% too high on foreign parts)** | ✅ 2026-07-14 verified | After the NOA catch, owner: "verify ALL agents implement the price policy and the client sees policy-correct prices." Audited every customer-price surface. **All 8 `calculate_customer_price_from_ils` calls (chat agents, brands API, identify-from-image) already pass `local_vat_only=True`; checkout uses `get_supplier_vat_rate`; NOA fixed.** The **one outlier: `_customer_price_fields` (the price shown on every SEARCH result) applied a FLAT 18% VAT** to every supplier — so a foreign-sourced part (Car-Parts.ie/IE etc.) **DISPLAYED ~18% higher than checkout actually charged** (confirmed live: Car-Parts.ie part search ₪115 vs checkout ₪98). Root fix: `_customer_price_fields` now takes `supplier_name/country` and applies the CONDITIONAL VAT via `get_supplier_vat_rate` (18% local, 0% foreign), matching checkout; wired supplier country into all 3 call sites (main search results, extra-suppliers block, comparison endpoint); unknown-supplier fallback keeps flat VAT so VAT is never silently dropped. **Verified:** foreign IE → total ₪97 (VAT 0, = checkout); local IL → ₪115 (VAT ₪17.49). Now the displayed price equals the charged price on every surface (G2). |
| **Imported DB prices audit — landed correctly per policy** | ✅ 2026-07-14 verified | Owner: "check imported prices landed correctly per policy." Sampled 20K IL-priced parts (TABLESAMPLE, bounded): **99% have `base_price = importer_price_ils × 1.45` (±2%)**, 0% at raw cost (margin missing), 0 below cost, 0 with double-VAT/over-margin. Import formula is correctly applied. (The earlier NOA "near-cost" case was `base_price` tied to the IL-importer cost while the cheapest SUPPLIER was foreign/lower — handled by NOA now pricing off the cheapest supplier, not base_price.) |
| **Postgres heavy-scan / CPU assessment (owner: batch the scans)** | ✅ 2026-07-14 assessed | The 29-min `UPDATE parts_catalog` + stacked `count()` that overloaded the box: the counts were **blocked waiting on the UPDATE's lock**, not 7 parallel scans. Audited the maintenance tasks — the heavy ones are **already batched/delta-bounded**: normalize_categories & normalize_part_types (bounded batches + `FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED` + per-batch `statement_timeout`), dedup_catalog_parts (delta mode — only recently-changed SKUs, not full-table), task6 category rollup (id-cursor batches), heal_* / task_normalize_base_price (batched), merge_catalog_fitment (per-manufacturer). | **NOA published prices ignored the local-only VAT condition (owner catch)** | ✅ 2026-07-14 verified | Owner: "verify NOA publishes prices per policy, not raw." Found NOA's marketing grounding (`_noa_real_catalog_fact`) priced as **flat `base_price × 1.18`** — two policy violations: (1) **VAT is conditional** — `get_supplier_vat_rate` applies 18% ONLY to LOCAL (IL) suppliers, **0% to foreign-sourced parts** (Car-Parts.ie/IE 563K, SNG/UK, eBay/US = a huge share of the catalog); the flat ×1.18 **overstated every foreign part by 18%**. (2) `base_price` is unreliable on some rows (≈ raw cost), so `base_price×1.18` could land near cost — a near-raw-cost display. Root fix: price from the **cheapest available supplier's real cost** (same source as website search/checkout) via the **canonical formula** — `cost × PROFIT_MARGIN(1.45)` then `+ VAT` from `get_supplier_vat_rate(supplier_name, country)`; label "כולל מע\"מ" vs "ללא מע\"מ (יבוא)". **Verified:** local IL supplier → ₪171 (18% VAT); foreign IE/UK/US → ₪145 (no VAT); price ALWAYS ≥ cost×1.45 (margin always applied, never raw cost). **Rule: NOA must price via cheapest-supplier cost × canonical conditional-VAT — never flat ×1.18, never base_price.** |
