| **Email template links audit (owner: "verify the links in the mail templates")** | ✅ 2026-07-14 verified + fixed | Verified every link across all 13 templates. **Static links all resolve (HTTP 200):** site root, `/parts`, `/login`, `logo.png`, all 4 `/email-icons/*.png`; contact links `wa.me/972532426920` (platform's own WA number) + `t.me/Noa_autosparefinder_bot` (matches NOA's platform handle) + `mailto:support@`. **Auth links valid:** `verify_email` → `GET /api/v1/auth/verify-email?token=` (backend route exists; HMAC token round-trips, tampered rejected); `password_reset` → `/reset-password?token=` (route exists); `missing_details` → `/profile` (route exists AND has phone+address+city fields). **BUG FOUND & FIXED — 6 templates deep-linked to non-existent detail routes.** The SPA (react-router v6.30) has only exact `path="/orders"` and `path="/parts"` plus a catch-all `path="*" → <Navigate to="/">`; there is NO `/orders/:id` or `/parts/:id` route, so `/orders/{id}` and `/parts/{id}` fell through to the catch-all and **redirected to the homepage**. Affected: order_confirmation, payment_received, invoice, delivery_update, review_request (all `→ {site}/orders/{id}`) and price_drop (`→ {site}/parts/{id}`). Fix: order emails now link to the real `/orders` list route (payments.py:198, admin.py:3077/3081, utils.py:448); price_drop links to the real search route `/parts?search=<url-encoded part name>` (BACKEND_API_ROUTES.py, which Parts.jsx reads via `?search=`). All 4 files compile; no remaining `/orders/{` or `/parts/{id}` email links. (Note: `parts.py:3564 supplier_url` uses the same non-existent `/parts/{id}` pattern as an internal data field — same root, out of email scope, flagged for follow-up.) |
| **Abandoned-cart link opened the wrong person's cart — recipient-scoped recovery link (owner: "route to the recipient's cart, not mine")** | ✅ 2026-07-16 verified live | The abandoned-cart email/WhatsApp fell back to a bare `{site}/cart` when no single-item `/pay/` link existed. The cart is **server-side per authenticated user** (`cartApi.get()`), so a generic `/cart` shows whoever is logged into that browser — the owner saw their OWN cart when testing. Fix: a **signed cart-recovery link** that logs the RECIPIENT into their own account and lands them on their cart. **Backend:** `create_cart_recovery_token`/`verify_cart_recovery_token` (HMAC over user_id+exp, keyed by JWT_SECRET_KEY, **purpose-scoped `'cart'`** so it can't be swapped with the email-verify token, 72h TTL) + `GET /api/v1/customers/cart/recover` (rate-limited; verifies token → mints a normal revocable session via `create_session` → 302 to `{site}/cart#ar=<JWT>&rt=<JWT>`). Tokens are handed to the SPA in the URL **fragment** (never sent to server logs/Referer). **Frontend:** `main.jsx` consumes `#ar/#rt` before React mounts — stores the tokens, **clears any other user's cached `auth-store`/`cart-store`** (so it never shows the wrong identity/cart), strips the fragment. `_cart_recovery_url()` now used as the fallback in `_abandoned_cart_loop` for BOTH email and WhatsApp (single-item still gets the direct `/pay/` one-tap). **Security posture:** issues a full 7-day-refresh session (standard cart-recovery UX, consistent with the existing auth-less `/pay/` links); token TTL 72h bounds a forwarded email; can be tightened to a no-refresh short session if desired. **Verified live:** bad/expired token → `302 /login?cart=expired`; valid token → `302 /cart#ar=…&rt=…`; the issued JWT authenticates as the **exact recipient** via `/auth/me`; email-verify token rejected as a cart token; frontend consumer present in the deployed bundle. |
| **New-user registration UI/UX + brand-identity pass (owner request)** | ✅ 2026-07-16 built + deployed | Rewrote `Register.jsx` on the established design system (brand tokens `#1ca7ff`/navy `#0b1f3a`, Rubik/Heebo, `.auth-panel`/`.input-field`/`.btn-primary`, `AuthBrandHeader`+`BrandLogo`). **UX:** live inline per-field validation (email/phone/name/password-match) with touched-state so errors show after interaction not on load; **brand-blue→success password-strength meter** (3 segments) replacing the plain bullet list; inline green check-marks on valid email/phone/confirm; submit shows precise error; a11y (`htmlFor`/`id`, `aria-invalid`, `autoComplete`, aria-labeled show-password). **Brand/i18n:** fixed the English "OR" divider → Hebrew "או"; replaced generic `text-gray-*` with slate/brand tokens; added a "פחות מדקה, בלי כרטיס אשראי" subhead + an encryption trust line. **Success state redesigned** to reflect BOTH post-register signals we now send — the SMS 2FA code AND the welcome/verify email — branded with the logo. Auth flow (`register()` → success → 2FA) unchanged. Frontend rebuilt + deployed. |
| **VIN decoder wired into the chat brain (owner request, from photo test)** | ✅ 2026-07-13 verified | The media test found the bot recognized a VIN but didn't decode it (asked make/model/year manually). A VIN decoder already existed (`_decode_vin_with_resilience` → NHTSA vPIC, cached + circuit-broken) but wasn't used by the chat flow. Added `_extract_vin_from_text` (17-char A-HJ-NPR-Z0-9, must mix letters+digits so a 17-digit run isn't a false VIN) + a VIN-decode block in `process_user_message` that drops make/model/year into `context_data` — which the existing free-text capture then treats exactly like a customer-stated car (no duplicate confirm logic). Works whether the VIN is typed OR read from a windshield/chassis photo by vision. NHTSA returns make+year reliably even for EU VINs (model can be blank → make+year still seeds the fitment flow). **Verified live:** `WVWZZZ6EZ5B002227` → VOLKSWAGEN/2005, `WVGZZZ5NZJM131395` → VOLKSWAGEN/2018; "VIN + צריך מסנן שמן" → bot: "מחפש מסנן שמן עבור ה‑Volkswagen 2018 עם VIN…" (was: "share make/model/year"). Detector rejects 17-digit strings. |
| **Chatbot media (photo + voice) test + Whisper vocabulary hint (owner request)** | ✅ 2026-07-13 (voice verified; image pixel-run pending files) | Tested the WA/TG/web media handling with real data. Pipeline: image→`hf_vision` (Gemini 2.0 Flash), voice→`hf_audio` (Groq Whisper)→`process_user_message` — all 3 channels share it. **Fix:** `hf_audio` now passes a Whisper **`prompt` vocabulary hint** (`_AUDIO_VOCAB_PROMPT`, env `WHISPER_VOCAB_PROMPT`) of Hebrew part terms + IL car models — no forced language so English still works. **Verified it fixed the exact mis-hearings found pre-fix:** `מצת` (spark plug) was heard `מצאת` → now `מצת`; `קיה ספורטג'` was `קיאס` → now 100%. **Voice results (8 wide scenarios, real TTS clips):** transcription ~8/8 usable, brain on-target 7/8, human + fitment-first on every channel; the brain captured vehicles from speech (`toyota/corolla/2016`, `honda/civic/2019`, Hyundai i35) and returned matching parts; multi-part EN request got a smart "front or rear?" clarify. **Photo-scenario brain test (owner's 9 real photos): part-search 5/5** on-target (AC compressor, ABS pump, Berlingo mirror, shock absorber, gearbox) — even distinguished BMW 320 E90 vs F30. **Findings:** (1) **VIN photos are recognized but NOT decoded** — bot acknowledges the VIN then asks make/model/year manually; adding a VIN decoder (VIN→make/model/year auto-fill) is a real opportunity. (2) **Pixel→ID vision on the owner's 12 real photos — RAN & verified 2026-07-13.** ~8/12 spot-on: AC compressor (worn) `מדחס מזגן` conf0.99, side mirror **from a shop screenshot** `מראה שמאל`, 3× VIN plates correctly flagged `VIN` conf1.0, ABS unit `מנוע ABS`, gearbox internals `תיבת הילוכים`, struts `בולם זעזועים`. The 3 in-situ dirty engine-bay shots → named the prominent air-intake/radiator hose in frame rather than the small hidden target sensor (honest limitation of "which part?" in a crowded mounted photo). **Resilience bonus:** Gemini was 429-rate-limited and its circuit tripped on image 1 → the **GROQ Llama-4-scout vision fallback handled all 12 accurately** — the fallback path is solid. Every ID fed the live search and got a fitment-first reply. **Foreground-part prompt tweak (2026-07-13, applied to all 3 vision prompts — chat.py, parts.py, webhooks.py caption):** instructs the model, when several parts are visible (component still mounted in the bay), to identify the CENTER/FOREGROUND part the photo is framed on — not the biggest hose/duct/cover in the background — and to lower confidence + list foreground alternatives when crowded, for a follow-up. **Verified improvement on re-run:** the two in-situ engine-bay shots that previously returned "air/radiator hose" now return **oxygen sensor** (alts O2/lambda) and **air-intake sensor** (alts MAP/IAT) — the actual foreground target; clean shots (compressor, mirror, VINs, ABS, struts) unchanged. Harnesses: `_media_chat_test.py`, `_photo_scenarios_test.py`, `_vision_folder_test.py`. |
| **2FA WhatsApp code — delete-for-everyone after successful verify (owner request)** | ✅ 2026-07-13 verified | Owner wanted the WhatsApp code message removed from the chat once it's used (keeps copy-paste + zero friction, unlike view-once). **Bridge** (`whatsapp-bridge/index.js`): `/send` now returns the sent-message `key` (non-breaking add); new **`/delete`** endpoint does `sock.sendMessage(jid,{delete:key})` (delete-for-everyone). **Provider** (`whatsapp_provider.py`): `send_message` returns the `key`; new `delete_message(key)`. **Auth** (`BACKEND_AUTH_SECURITY.py`): `send_whatsapp_2fa` returns `{ok,key}`; `create_2fa_code` stores the key on the 2FA row (new `two_factor_codes.wa_message_key` TEXT column); `verify_2fa_code` deletes the message **best-effort AFTER** marking verified. **No-damage guarantees:** delete is wrapped in try/except and runs post-commit — a failed delete NEVER affects login; SMS-fallback rows have no key → nothing to delete; invalid/missing key → bridge 400, ignored. **Verified:** `/delete` validates (400 on empty key, sends nothing); bridge reconnected + `/health` connected; full backend round-trip — key stored, correct code returns True *while the (intentionally-invalid-JID) delete failed non-fatally*, wrong code returns False. Real send→key→delete exercised on the next real login. |
| **2FA security hardening — hash code at rest + quiet Twilio logger (owner request)** | ✅ 2026-07-13 verified | Owner asked to secure the 2FA delivery. **(1) Confirmed safe already:** WhatsApp content is E2E-encrypted (Baileys = real WhatsApp client, Signal protocol → WhatsApp Inc. can't read it); audit found the code is NOT logged anywhere (backend prints don't include it, provider doesn't log text, bridge logs `text length` only + doesn't persist message bodies). **(2) Code hashed at rest:** `two_factor_codes.code` was plaintext `varchar(6)` — widened to `varchar(128)` and now stores an **HMAC-SHA256** (`hash_2fa_code`, keyed by `JWT_SECRET_KEY` so a DB-only breach can't brute-force the 10^6 space). `create_2fa_code` stores the hash (raw code still sent to the user); `verify_2fa_code` uses constant-time `_2fa_code_matches` with a legacy-plaintext fallback (len==6) so codes issued just before deploy still verify until they expire. **(3) Quieted the Twilio SDK logger** (`twilio.http_client` INFO→WARNING) — it was dumping full request/response incl. the destination phone number to docker logs on every SMS (the code itself was never in the body, but the phone was). Verified: 64-hex hash, correct verifies / wrong rejected / legacy works, logger level=WARNING, app healthy. |
| **2FA via WhatsApp (Baileys bridge) — WhatsApp-primary, SMS fallback (owner request)** | ✅ 2026-07-13 live (awaiting owner login test) | Owner wanted 2FA codes delivered over WhatsApp (via the existing Baileys bridge) instead of paying for Twilio. Infra already existed: `social/whatsapp_provider.py` IS the Baileys bridge (`whatsapp-bridge:3001/send`, container up 8d, same path `_wa_send` uses for owner alerts), and a disabled `send_whatsapp_2fa`. Wired **WHATSAPP_2FA_PRIMARY** mode in `create_2fa_code`: try WhatsApp first (free); **fall back to Twilio SMS only if WhatsApp fails** (bridge down / number not on WhatsApp) so nobody is ever locked out. `send_whatsapp_2fa` gate widened to honor the new flag; `WHATSAPP_2FA_PRIMARY=1` + `WHATSAPP_BRIDGE_URL` added to compose. Same RTL-clean message on both channels. **⚠️ RISK (told owner):** sending OTP over an UNOFFICIAL WhatsApp (Baileys) violates WhatsApp ToS and can get the platform number BANNED — which would kill ALL WhatsApp features (customer chat, alerts, 2FA). SMS fallback is retained precisely so a ban/outage never locks users out; recommended NOT removing Twilio entirely. Reversible: set `WHATSAPP_2FA_PRIMARY=0`. |
| **Backend bind-mount MISSING from compose — silent deploy drift (INFRA)** | ✅ 2026-07-13 restored | Discovered while enabling WhatsApp-2FA: a `docker compose up -d backend` recreate **reverted the backend code to a stale image**, dropping live edits (the exact drift CLAUDE.md said was fixed on 2026-06-30 by bind-mounting `./backend:/app`). Reality: that bind-mount was NOT in the current `docker-compose.yml` (only `upload_files`/`worker_state` volumes). Earlier `docker restart`s appeared to pick up edits only because the long-running PREVIOUS container was created back when the mount existed; the recreate applied the current (mount-less) compose and fell back to the image. **Fix:** restored `- ./backend:/app` to the backend service `volumes:` (deps still come from the image's site-packages; sub-path volumes override uploads/state). Verified: container `/app` now == host `backend/`, all live edits active, app healthy. **Deploys are safe again — but always confirm the mount exists before trusting `docker restart` to pick up edits.** |
| **2FA SMS broken for ALL users — `twilio` package missing (CRITICAL)** | ✅ 2026-07-13 fixed + verified (owner received SMS) | Discovered while verifying the logo fix: login triggered 2FA, owner got **no SMS**. Root cause: Twilio creds were all configured (SID/token/`TWILIO_PHONE_NUMBER=+18704084670`) but the **`twilio` Python package was never installed** (absent from `requirements.txt`) — so `send_sms_2fa`'s `from twilio.rest import Client` threw `ModuleNotFoundError`, was swallowed by the `except`, returned False, and **no code was ever sent to anyone**. No WhatsApp fallback either (`ENABLE_WHATSAPP_2FA` unset). Blast radius: every user with 2FA (incl. owner) locked out. Fix: `pip install twilio` (9.10.9) in the running container — takes effect immediately since `send_sms_2fa` imports at call-time, no restart needed; added `twilio>=9.0,<10.0` to `requirements.txt`; rebuilt the backend image so it stays baked in on recreate. Phone normalization verified (`0586050155`→`+972586050155`). **Verified: owner received the SMS.** Recommended follow-up (owner's call): set `ENABLE_WHATSAPP_2FA=1` for a redundant channel so a Twilio hiccup can't lock everyone out again. |
| **2FA SMS text — warm/welcoming rewrite + CS-chat link (owner request)** | ✅ 2026-07-13 live | Default `TWO_FA_MESSAGE_TEMPLATE` rewritten from a terse code notice to a warm, welcoming Hebrew message that links to the **CS chat** (owner chose CS chat over the site link — a login-free channel a stuck-at-login user can actually reach): "היי {name} 👋 ברוכים הבאים ל-AutoSpare — חלקי חילוף לרכב בקלות. / קוד האימות שלך: {code} (בתוקף {minutes} דקות). / צריכים עזרה? דברו איתנו: {support} / הקוד אישי לך בלבד, נא לא לשתף אותו 🔒". `{support}` = `TWO_FA_SUPPORT_URL` → defaults to the platform WhatsApp `https://wa.me/972532426920` (falls back through `NOA_WHATSAPP_URL`); override env for a Telegram/web-chat link instead. `_build_2fa_message` passes `support=` (and keeps `site=` for back-compat with any custom template); dropped the orphan brand-icon line (`TWO_FA_BRAND_ICON` default → ""). Fully env-overridable. **Trimmed to 2 UCS-2 (Hebrew) SMS segments** (126 UTF-16 units w/ a normal name; a 14-char name still = 134 ≤ 2 seg) per owner request — dropped the tagline + 2nd emoji, shortened phrases, kept the warm greeting + CS link + security note. Final: "היי {name} 👋 ברוכים הבאים ל-AutoSpare! / קוד האימות: {code} (בתוקף {minutes} דק'). / עזרה: {support} / קוד אישי, לא לשתף." Verified live render = 2 segments. **RTL fix (2026-07-13):** owner reported the received SMS looked "messy" — mixed Hebrew (RTL) + code/URL/"AutoSpare" (LTR) was scrambled by the phone's bidi algorithm. Fix in `_build_2fa_message`: prefix every line with RLM (`‏`) to pin RTL base direction, and wrap the URL in LRM (`‎`) so the LTR run stays intact (`_rtl_lines()` helper). Still 2 segments (132 units). Awaiting owner confirmation of on-device rendering. |

## Session — 2026-07-11 (RockAuto price pipeline — browser-harvest, like car-parts.ie)

Owner directive: close the price gap with wider sources. RockAuto (best match for the US-OEM catalog) blocks server-side scraping (FlareSolverr + Playwright both fail — confirmed), so built the same BROWSER-HARVEST pipeline that works for car-parts.ie: owner's real browser extracts {oem, price} → relays to `/api/v1/system/collect` (brand=`rockauto`) → importer fills prices by matching normalized OEM to existing unpriced parts.

| Piece | Status | Detail |
|------|--------|--------|
| `rockauto_price_import.py` | ✅ built + tested (all-options) | Groups all brand options RockAuto returns per OEM. supplier_parts allows only ONE row per (part_id, supplier_id), so it stores the CHEAPEST option as the RockAuto buy-offer (headline, for cross-supplier comparison) AND records the FULL options list in `parts_catalog.specifications->'rockauto_options'` (owner: "import all prices, not just cheapest"). Uses the LIVE FX rate from system_settings (3.01, REX-refreshed — not hardcoded), stamps US→Israel shipping = **$57.99 × live rate ≈ ₪174.55 (MEASURED, not invented — see below)**, fills catalog price only when unpriced. Verified: 3-option payload → 1 offer (cheapest ₪7.28), base_price ₪10.56, 3 options in specs. |
| **RockAuto shipping fee — corrected from fabricated ₪110 to MEASURED $57.99** | ✅ 2026-07-11 | Owner caught that the ₪110 RockAuto (and ₪95 eBay Motors, ₪39 AliExpress) shipping rates I added were **invented placeholders, never verified** — violates the real-data-only rule. Opened the live RockAuto cart (OEM `04152-YZZA1` → Tel Aviv, IL), added the part, filled a real IL address, clicked Calculate Shipping: RockAuto quoted **$57.99 Economy (recv Mon Jul 20) / $61.99 Expedited (recv Thu Jul 16)**, order total $60.41 on a $2.42 part. Key facts: (1) shipping is a **PER-ORDER international floor (~$58)**, weight-scales above it, NOT per-part — so cheap single-part US→IL orders are uneconomic and parts must be consolidated per order; (2) RockAuto listing/search pages expose **no** shipping — only the cart with a ship-to address does. **Fix:** `SUPPLIER_SHIPPING_RATES["RockAuto"]` and `rockauto_price_import.py` now use `$57.99 × live USD→ILS rate` (≈₪174.55, currency-tracking, env `ROCKAUTO_SHIP_USD`). **Removed** the fabricated `eBay Motors`/`AliExpress` flat entries entirely — eBay's real per-item shipping already flows from the API (`ebay_price_sync.py`: `selected.shipping_cost → shipping_cost_ils` on supplier_parts); a hardcoded flat rate was both fake and redundant. Verified live: `SUPPLIER_SHIPPING_RATES['RockAuto']=174.55`, eBay/AliExpress=None. |
| **Shipping config audit — pruned 8 FICTIONAL suppliers + measured car-parts.ie** | ✅ 2026-07-12 | Applied "measure, don't guess" to the whole `SUPPLIER_SHIPPING_RATES` table. DB check: **8 of 11 supplier names were pure fiction** (`Global Parts Hub`, `EastAuto Supply`, `PartsPro USA`, `AutoZone Direct`, `Hyundai Mobis`, `Kia Parts Direct`, `Bosch Direct`, `Toyota Genuine`) — **0 suppliers / 0 parts each** in the DB, invented rates that never matched a real seller. Removed. Kept only the 3 real ones: `AutoParts Pro IL` (115k parts, IL-domestic ₪29, labeled estimate), `RockAuto` (measured ₪174.55), and eBay/AliExpress deliberately absent (real API shipping). `_COUNTRY_SHIPPING_RATES` re-labeled as **unverified last-resort estimates** (not measured quotes). **MEASURED live in browser: car-parts.ie — our BIGGEST supplier (560,951 parts) — does NOT ship to Israel at all** (their /shipping page: "We ship from locations within the EU", lists only EU+UK/NO/CH; no IL). Cheapest EU leg DE €6.95 / IE €17.95. So the ₪59 default shown for those 561k parts is fiction — they need an EU-hub freight-forwarder (two-leg) before honest pricing; flagged for a real forwarder quote / owner decision. Durable fix = capture-at-harvest per part (like eBay's `shipping_cost_ils`), not better table guesses. |
| **BMW duplicate-OEM dedup — FK-safe batched soft-delete** | ✅ 2026-07-12 (DONE: 36,760 groups, 69,063 rows retired, 63,893 sp + 46,137 fitment repointed, 0 orphans verified) | Root cause (long-documented, deferred): OEMPartsOnline imported BMW OEMs WITH dashes, Delek WITHOUT, so the same part exists as 2+ active catalog rows. Live count: **36,760 dup groups, 69,058 removable rows**, carrying **63,782 supplier_parts + 70,913 fitment rows** — must be REPOINTED, never orphaned. Built `bmw_oem_dedup.py`: per normalized-OEM group, canonical = priced row (else lowest id); repoints supplier_parts to canonical (keeps CHEAPER price on `(part_id,supplier_id)` conflict, else moves), repoints fitment (drops exact dups), then **SOFT-deletes** losers (`is_active=FALSE` + `specifications.dedup_merged_into=<canonical>` — fully reversible, nothing hard-deleted). Safety: one-time work-table materializes the dup groups (avoids re-scanning 339K rows every batch — the timeout cause), then batches of 200 with `FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED` (never fights the harvester), claim+merge in ONE tx (rollback un-claims), deadlock-retry, `SET LOCAL statement_timeout`. Verified on 10 groups: 20 losers soft-deleted, 20 sp + 8 fitment repointed, canonical active with all FKs, losers emptied, **0 orphans**. Full run executing in background. Reusable for any brand: `--brand <x>`. |
| **ClamAV container down — hardware-incompatible (RAM), needs owner decision** | ⏳ decision | `autospare_clamav` up-but-unhealthy for ~4 weeks: freshclam DB load **OOM-killed (signal 9)** and clamd itself never loads (using 10MB of a 512MB cap; modern ClamAV needs ~1.2–2GB just to load signatures). Box has ~1.4GB free, **no swap** → giving ClamAV the RAM it needs risks OOM-killing Postgres/backend. Upload scan (`routes/utils.py:_scan_bytes_for_virus`) **fails OPEN** (returns `skipped` when clamd is unreachable), so uploads still work unscanned — no functional breakage, only missing AV on the auth-gated owner-upload path. Not a code bug; a genuine RAM tradeoff. Options: (A) decommission ClamAV, reclaim 512MB, keep fail-open — recommended given the box; (B) provision more RAM; (C) leave broken. Awaiting owner call. |
| **Amayama harvester → SERVER-SIDE via FlareSolverr (owner directive: off the Chrome MCP)** | ✅ 2026-07-13 | Owner wanted the Amayama harvest on the server, not the Chrome-MCP browser, using our existing FlareSolverr — and was RIGHT that FlareSolverr can do it (I'd wrongly concluded it couldn't). **The technique that makes Amayama's Cloudflare clear server-side:** a one-off `request.get` on a deep `/part/` URL hits a HARD challenge and times out — but **`sessions.create` → warm on the homepage (CF clears for the session) → then fetch `/en/search?q=<oem>` with `maxTimeout=120000`** returns HTTP 200 with the part table. My first Amayama test failed only because it was cold (no session, no warmup, 60s) — my mistake, not a FlareSolverr limitation (car-parts.ie uses this exact session flow). **Login for prices:** Amayama serves the catalog anonymously but renders prices+IL-shipping only when logged in — the account session cookie (`ama_ssid_s`) is injected via FlareSolverr's `cookies` param from `amayama_session.json` (chmod 600, owner-provided; cf_clearance NOT needed — FlareSolverr clears CF itself, and it's IP-bound anyway). **Self-API 403 fix:** feed/collect calls go out through Cloudflare, which 403s urllib's default UA — added a Chrome UA (same fix as the car-parts.ie relay). **Verified live end-to-end:** logged_in=True, prices match the browser exactly ($9.82+$28.56 JP / $17.99+$34.51 UAE); round 1 = 16/40 OEMs matched, 24 priced rows relayed → landed in DB (₪146.77+₪122 ship, etc). `amayama_flaresolverr_harvester.py` + supervisor `_amayama_fs_harvester_loop` (auto-restart, skips fast if no cookie) — no installs, no paid services, no Chrome MCP. **NOTE: `ama_ssid_s` expires — when it does, refresh amayama_session.json (or set env creds for auto-login).** |
| **G6 Categorization root-fix — category_map engine + backfill + pipeline + lock-storm fix** | ✅ 2026-07-13 (deployed; backfill running) | Owner goal: no part in `general`, all in the correct category, fast queries. **Root cause:** new imports didn't categorize on ingest + the ruleset was too thin for the real vocabulary → drift to 1.09M `כללי` + 776K `general`. **Data-grounded (not guessing):** backlog = 58% oempartsonline (real English names) + Car-Parts.ie 58K (**category = last URL path segment**, 186 slugs) + IL importers. **Built:** (1) `category_map.py` — single source of truth: VARIANT_MAP (folds ~70 messy labels like "Brakes"/"Oil Filters"/"Doors" → 22 canonical slugs) + URL-slug + comprehensive keyword RULES (specific→generic, multi-word disambiguation so a wrong category can't fire — a wrong cat is worse than `general`). Backlog match rate 14%→22% keyword, + OEM-twin ~8%. (2) `recategorize_backlog.py` — lock-safe batched backfill (Pass A variants, B keyword/slug, C OEM-twin inheritance; `FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED` + `lock_timeout`). (3) **Pipeline wired:** `db_cleanup_agent._guess_category` now tries `category_map` first → NEW parts auto-categorize; `car_parts_ie_import_generic._map_category` falls back to `category_map` (fixes plural/singular slug mismatches — map had "shock-absorber**s**", URLs use "shock-absorber" — that dropped parts to service-general). (4) **`normalize_part_types` bounded-batching** — was ONE 27-min UPDATE holding parts_catalog locks (the lock storm that blocked categorization/importers); now chunks of 5000, per-batch commit, SKIP LOCKED. **Honest ceiling:** deterministic+keyword safely categorizes ~30% of the low-info backlog ("Support"/"Shim"/"Key Master" are un-keyword-able); the rest is closed over time by the EXISTING `task3b_llm_category_fallback` (LLM), now fed the smaller residual. Index: btree `ix_parts_catalog_category` adequate for filtering. (verification + deploy pending) |
| **Categorization backlog investigated + categorizer made lock-safe (~10-14% keyword ceiling)** | ✅ 2026-07-13 | Rescan found categorization drifted (99.8%@570K → **1.09M `כללי` + 776K `general`** @4.1M). Running `categorize_parts_batch.py` surfaced TWO things: (1) **a live lock storm** — a `normalize_part_types` UPDATE (db_update_agent) ran **27+ min** holding parts_catalog locks, blocking the category-normalizer, enrichment, the car-parts.ie importer AND the categorizer (chain of 5-27min waiters). Made the categorizer **lock-safe**: per-batch tx with `FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED` + `SET LOCAL lock_timeout='4s'` + retry, so it never blocks/gets-blocked (skips locked rows, catches them next pass). (2) **The backlog is low-information parts** — measured true keyword match rate **~10-14%**; the rest are short/generic names ("Rack", "Lid Cognac", "Cover Black Deep Sea Blue", "Hx Nut") that can't be categorized without GUESSING (forbidden by fitment-first/no-wrong-data). `specifications.category_path` exists but is populated on only **0.2%**. So keyword categorization tops out ~10-14% (~130K real); the rest honestly stay `general`. Categorizer running to completion. **Deeper fix (separate project): re-harvest car-parts.ie WITH its page category, or LLM categorization** — not forced keyword matching. **Also surfaced: normalize_part_types needs bounded batching (it single-UPDATEs for 27min, serializing all catalog writes).** |
| **Amayama harvester supervisor + heartbeat-based alerting** | ✅ 2026-07-13 | Owner got a false "idle" alert while the tab was still open — the monitor wrongly equated "no new parts" with "tab closed" (the real cause was a Cloudflare rate-limit penalty from a concurrency speed-test: output paused but the harvester was still running). Fix = **heartbeat, not output**: new shared `harvest_heartbeat.py` (in-process dict); the feed endpoint records `record(source)` on every call, so a harvester hitting the feed = ALIVE even when Cloudflare blocks its output. Monitor rewritten to alert ONLY when `heartbeat_age > 25 min` (genuinely stopped / tab closed) with a 4h cooldown — never on idle output. Plus a **browser-side supervisor** `AMY.supervise()` that auto-restarts the self-healing loop on any crash (loop already retries 403/429 with backoff + adaptive delay; Amayama MUST stay sequential — concurrency 8/3/2 → 91/48/67% blocked). Verified: feed hit logs 200 + heartbeat records `source=amayama`; harvest recovered to 377 parts and self-supervises. (Note: a console-injected script still dies if the TAB is closed — a Tampermonkey userscript would survive that; offered as the fully-hands-off option.) |
| **AliExpress data-integrity audit + bulletproof OEM guard** | ✅ 2026-07-12 | Owner red-flagged: if AliExpress data is fuzzy, re-check that nothing wrong was imported. **Audit result: AliExpress `supplier_parts` = 0** — the sync writes a supplier_part AND touches `min_price_ils` ONLY after a match, and the old code (numeric-product_id-only search) matched 0 OEMs, so it wrote NOTHING anywhere. Catalog is clean of AliExpress data; `base_price` was never in the write path (only `min_price_ils`); live-search AliExpress results are ephemeral (Redis, never persisted). **Hardening (defense-in-depth):** the guarded keyword search's substring check could theoretically false-match a short OEM (`12345` inside `123456`). Replaced with `_oem_matches_title()` — requires len≥8 AND a **token-boundary** match on the raw title (OEM as-is / no-separator / dash→space forms), so a wrong part can never be attached. Unit-verified 7/7 (rejects too-short, embedded-in-longer-run, absent; accepts only genuine bounded matches). For a fuzzy source, correctness beats recall — it will keep writing ~0, safely. |
| **International seller harvest A+B+C — match-rate is brand-home-market dependent** | ✅ 2026-07-12 | Ran the 3 requested source builds against REAL unpriced OEMs. **Core insight: our unpriced catalog is region-specific OEMs, so a source only matches parts from ITS home market.** **A) AliExpress** — root-caused the 0-harvest: `search()` required a numeric AliExpress product_id and skipped all OEMs. Added a safe **guarded keyword search** (`aliexpress.ds.text.search` + strict OEM-must-be-in-title guard so we never attach a wrong part's price — fitment-first). But the DS "selection" feed is fuzzy/category-based: **0/10 popular OEMs** carried the OEM in any title → AliExpress is NOT a productive OEM source (code is safe + future-proof, just low yield). **B) RockAuto** — browser harvester scaled via `autorun` on US-brand unpriced OEMs → **~0 matched** (our Toyota/Honda unpriced OEMs are IL-market variants, not in RockAuto's US catalog; only US-market OEMs like the earlier 04152-YZZA1 hit). **C) Amayama** — the winner: OEM-precise Japanese/Asian catalog, Cloudflare-blocked server-side so browser-harvested (owner's logged-in account, ship-to-IL). Built `amayama_price_import.py` + collect route (`brand='amayama'`) + `amayama_browser_harvester.js`. Amayama part pages expose **part price AND real IL shipping per offer** (`.part-price` / `.shipping_price`). **Verified live: 4/15 (~27%) unpriced OEMs matched** → priced with real shipping (e.g. ₪757.80+₪228 ship, ₪19.11+₪86 ship). Correctness guard: shipping=0 (parser miss) stored as NULL, never as false "free shipping". **Amayama's Customer API is order-tracking only** (no product/price endpoint) — so harvest is browser, fulfilment/tracking could use the API. **Scaled + supervised (2026-07-12):** a true server-side loop like car-parts.ie is NOT feasible — FlareSolverr can't bypass Amayama's Cloudflare (tested: error/0 bytes) AND it needs the owner's login for IL shipping. So delivered instead: (1) a **self-perpetuating browser `AMY.loop()`** (one command sweeps the whole Japanese-brand unpriced set continuously; the feed's rotating cursor tracks progress across sessions; `AMY.stop=true` halts); (2) a server-side supervisor `_amayama_harvest_monitor_loop()` (30-min: logs `amayama_parts`/delta/jp_unpriced; WhatsApps the owner to reopen the tab if throughput stalls ~1h while work remains — since it can't restart a browser). Verified climbing live: 4→16→37 priced parts. |
| **RockAuto browser pipeline — CORS wall + auth gap fixed, unpriced-OEM feed, verified E2E** | ✅ 2026-07-12 | Two blockers found by testing live in the browser: (1) `COLLECT_SECRET` is enforced but the browser relay sent no secret → 403; (2) more fundamentally, the cross-origin relay from rockauto.com → our backend **failed even to reach a readable state**: the global Starlette `CORSMiddleware` (allow_origins = our own domains only) **rejects the OPTIONS preflight with 400** for any other origin / non-safelisted header, so a `X-Collect-Secret` header (which forces a preflight) can never work and my per-route OPTIONS handler never runs. Confirmed via browser: no-cors POST resolved opaque (⇒ NOT CSP/network), OPTIONS → 400, GET → 403-without-ACAO. **Solution (no global CORS weakening):** use CORS **"simple requests"** — `POST` with `Content-Type: text/plain` and the **secret in the body** (no preflight); backend reads the secret from the body and returns `ACAO:*` so the browser can read the reply; `credentials:'omit'` keeps `ACAO:*` valid. Applied to BOTH `/collect` (relay) and the new **`/api/v1/system/unpriced-oems`** feed (GET for same-origin/server, POST-text/plain for the browser). Feed is authenticated (same secret — enumerating OEMs must never be open), returns unpriced active OEMs for US-market brands via a **rotating id cursor** (sweeps the whole catalog + wraps). `rockauto_browser_harvester.js`: `auth()` (paste-once, never in repo) + `feed()` + `autorun(rounds,batch)` one-click continuous. **Verified live end-to-end from a rockauto.com tab:** feed returned 12 unpriced OEMs cross-origin → bulk-harvested OEM `04152-YZZA1` (13 brand options) → relay `status:saved total:13 pid` → importer `matched=1 price_filled=1 rate=3.01` → DB: RockAuto offer `price_ils=7.28`, `shipping_cost_ils=174.55` (real measured), 13 options in `specifications.rockauto_options`, existing base_price preserved. **Data caveat:** our unpriced catalog OEMs are mostly Israel-market (suffixed Toyota/Honda/Nissan numbers) and 0/12 in the random sample existed in RockAuto's US catalog — RockAuto pays off for US-market OEMs, not region-specific ones; best harvested by browsing US vehicles on RockAuto directly. |
| collect route | ✅ live | `routes/system.py` routes `brand='rockauto'` harvests to the importer (alongside oempartsonline / car-parts.ie). |
| `rockauto_browser_harvester.js` | ✅ provided (needs field-test) | Console script for the owner's RockAuto tab. DRY-RUN first (prints a table of found {oem, price} without sending); `RA_HARVEST.send()` relays. Defensive extraction (finds `$xx.xx` price leaves, climbs to the listing container, pulls the nearest Part#/brand) — selectors need one real-page verification since RockAuto's DOM is obfuscated and blocked from server inspection. |
| TecDoc (TecAlliance) trial | ⏳ pending activation (~24h) | Owner registered at shop.tecalliance.net; awaiting account activation + API credentials. Will build the catalog importer (OEM cross-refs + fitment; NOT retail prices — but the cross-refs unlock more supplier price matches) once credentials land. |

## Session — 2026-07-11 (Price-sync tuning + supplier-source reality)

| Item | Status | Detail |
|------|--------|--------|
| **eBay sync aborted mid-run — `change_pct` overflow (ROOT FIX)** | ✅ | `price_history.change_pct` is `NUMERIC(7,4)` (max ±999.9999). A part re-priced from a stale near-zero old price computed e.g. **+4405%** → `NumericValueOutOfRangeError` → the eBay sync **aborted partway** (last run did 1358 of its 4400 budget). Capped `change_pct` to ±999.9999 in both `ebay_price_sync` and `aliexpress_price_sync` (it's a change indicator, not money). eBay now completes full 4400-part runs (~30% match ≈ ~1300 newly-priced/run). |
| **Match-rate reality (measured)** | ℹ️ | eBay: ~25–33% OEM match (32,693 parts priced, real). **AliExpress: 0% match** (2000 checked → 2000 not_found) — it doesn't carry US-OEM parts by OEM number; raising its limit only wastes quota (recommend lowering `ALIEXPRESS_PRICE_SYNC_LIMIT`). eBay is near its API ceiling (~5000/day), so the price gap **cannot be closed by eBay alone** — needs more OEM-focused price sources. |
| **Supplier-aggregator suppliers are affiliate LINK-only (gap)** | ⚠️ Open | The 15 wired suppliers (PartSouq/Amayama/Autodoc-display/RockAuto/FCP Euro/…) return **`price=0.0` click-through links**, not importable prices — only eBay & AliExpress return real API prices. To use them for pricing we must build REAL price connectors (API/scrape) for the price-bearing sites — **Autodoc** (broad EU OEM) and **RockAuto** (US OEM) are the best candidates. This is the real lever for the price gap. |

## Session — 2026-07-11 (Price gap — why 2.24M parts never got priced)

Owner asked to verify the 2.24M `base_price=0` parts aren't duplicates and why the price-update task isn't closing the gap.

| Item | Status | Detail |
|------|--------|--------|
| **Not duplicates — VERIFIED** | ✅ | Sampled 20K of the 2.1M oempartsonline (US OEM) unpriced parts: only **205 (~1%)** have a normalized-OEM twin that is priced. So they are NOT mostly duplicates — they're genuine unique parts imported for OEM + fitment + barcode coverage (as intended). The dedup-by-normalized-OEM concern is low priority (~1%). |
| **Root cause — the parts have NO price captured** | ✅ (diagnosed) | All sampled unpriced parts have a `supplier_parts` row but with `price_ils=0 AND price_usd=0`, and `specifications` holds only `source` — no price anywhere. The oempartsonline importer captures a price WHEN the source page has one (msrp/sale USD → price_ils → base_price×1.45), but these were imported when the price field was empty. To sell them we must SOURCE a price (eBay/AliExpress by OEM). |
| **Why the price-update task wasn't closing it (ROOT FIX)** | ✅ | `sync_ebay_prices` / `sync_aliexpress_prices` (run daily inside `sync_prices`) had two gap-defeating bugs: (1) **`ORDER BY RANDOM()` with NO `base_price=0` filter** → they spent their whole daily budget re-pricing ALREADY-priced parts, rarely touching an unpriced one; (2) eBay had a **24-brand whitelist** that excluded the LARGEST unpriced blocks — Kia (388K), Toyota (362K), Porsche (261K), Lexus (218K) etc. → **70% (1.57M) of unpriced parts could never get a price**. Also the `ORDER BY RANDOM()` over millions took **48s**. **Fix:** both syncs now target ONLY unpriced parts (`base_price IS NULL OR =0`) walked by a rotating **id cursor** (`_EBAY_SYNC_LAST_ID` / `_ALIEXPRESS_SYNC_LAST_ID`) — cheap (pkey index, ~1.8s), deterministic forward progress, wraps at the end. eBay brand list expanded to cover the big unpriced brands (env-overridable `EBAY_PRICE_SYNC_BRANDS`, or `ALL`). Verified: selection now returns unpriced parts from Kia/Toyota/Porsche (previously excluded). |
| **Honest scale reality** | ⚠️ | Even fixed, throughput is eBay/AliExpress-API-limited (~700 parts/day) → 2.24M is a **multi-year** drain, and only parts eBay/AliExpress actually list by OEM will get a price (US-market OEM parts may not). This is partly the documented STRUCTURAL gap (IL importers don't stock these). The fix ensures every run now COUNTS toward the gap instead of wasting it; to go faster, raise `EBAY_PRICE_SYNC_LIMIT`/`ALIEXPRESS_PRICE_SYNC_LIMIT` within API tolerance or add more supplier sources. |

## Session — 2026-07-11 (Deadlock fix finished + bottleneck hunt)

| Item | Status | Detail |
|------|--------|--------|
| **Central deadlock-retry — VERIFIED** | ✅ | Proved the `run_all_tasks` retry resolves a real deadlock: injected a task that raises `DeadlockDetectedError` on attempt 1 and succeeds on attempt 2 → drove it through the real `run_task` dispatch + the exact retry loop → **calls=2, final_status=ok** (deadlock caught → retried → succeeded). Timeouts are still NOT retried. |
| **Deadlock source reduction** | ✅ | `dedup_catalog_parts` now sorts its id array before `UPDATE … WHERE id = ANY(:ids)` so row locks are taken in a consistent (id) order (fewer collisions with the harvester; the central retry is the backstop). `normalize_categories` Pass 2 already uses `ORDER BY id … FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED` (earlier this session). |
| **BOTTLENECK: `task_normalize_base_price_batched` — 90s+ scan every 30s (ROOT FIX)** | ✅ | db_cleanup_agent ran this every 30s; despite `LIMIT 1000` its inner query drove from `supplier_parts` and filtered for the rare `base_price=0` parts that also have a price — **found 0 fixable** (all 2.24M `base_price=0` parts are the unpriced global catalog with no supplier price) yet **Seq-scanned 2.24M rows** each run. This was the chronic snapshot-holder that **blocked a CONCURRENTLY index build and deadlocked the harvester** earlier this session. **Root fix:** a `base_price=0` part only becomes fixable when the harvester writes a supplier price → which bumps `supplier_parts.updated_at` (indexed by `ix_supplier_parts_updated_at`). Rewrote the inner query to drive from `supplier_parts WHERE updated_at > NOW()-INTERVAL '35 min'` → **0.21s** (was 90s+). Plus exponential backoff (30s→30min) when nothing fixable **and on timeout/error** (a shared `_bump_base_price_backoff()`), and `SET LOCAL statement_timeout='30s'` so it can never run long again. The 35-min window > 30-min max backoff → nothing missed. Verified: `fixed=0 elapsed=0.21s backed_off=True`. |
| **BOTTLENECK: `normalize_base_price` (db_update_agent) full-table scan** | ✅ | The other base-price task (disabled in the run_all_tasks list but callable) did 3 UPDATEs scanning the whole 1.9M-row priced table every run. Delta-scoped it (`(:full_pass OR updated_at > :since)` + checkpoint + weekly safety full pass + `lock_timeout='2min'`) — same pattern as `refresh_min_max_prices`. base_price only needs recomputing when a source price changed (which bumps updated_at). |

## Session — 2026-07-11 (Fitment search — query optimized with indexing)

The "browse all parts for my car" path (empty text query + vehicle fitment) took **~57s cold** per part-type (×3). EXPLAIN'd it and optimized the fitment filter with indexing.

| Item | Status | Detail |
|------|--------|--------|
| **Fitment EXISTS un-indexable → 57s scan** | ✅ | Root cause: the `part_vehicle_fitment` EXISTS in `_build_strict_vehicle_match_clause` (routes/parts.py) used `LOWER(TRIM(col)) = x OR LOWER(TRIM(col)) LIKE '%x%' OR :q LIKE '%'||col||'%'`. The leading-wildcard LIKE and the **reverse-substring** branch (`:q LIKE '%'||col||'%'`) can't use ANY btree, so the planner scanned the whole year-range and post-filtered (16s+ index scan, 57s total). **Fix — indexes + recall-preserving query rewrite:** (1) `pg_trgm` GIN indexes `idx_pvf_mfr_trgm` / `idx_pvf_model_trgm` on `lower(btrim(manufacturer/model))` → make `LIKE '%x%'` index-usable; btree `idx_pvf_model_norm (lower(btrim(model)), year_from, coalesce(year_to,year_from), part_id)`. (2) Rewrote the clause: **manufacturer** = equality + forward `LIKE '%x%'` (dropped the reverse branch — measured 0 added recall for Toyota/Mazda); **model** = `= ANY(<variant + its word-prefixes>)` (replaces the un-indexable reverse LIKE with an indexable IN — a specific customer model "Corolla Verso" still matches a general fitment "Corolla": **43,704 rows both ways**, recall preserved) + forward `LIKE 'x %'` (catches the opposite, "Corolla Cross"). **Result:** fitment filter isolated **56s → 6s** (EXPLAIN shows `idx_pvf_model_norm` BitmapAnd `idx_pvf_model_trgm` now drive it); recall verified on Corolla / Corolla Verso / Mazda 3 / i35 / Sportage (72 result-options each), text-query+vehicle searches 7–15s cold / ~0s warm. |
| **Remaining: full empty-query browse still ~40s cold** | ⚠️ Mitigated | After the fitment filter fix, the residual cold cost is `ORDER BY price_ils` **materializing + sorting all ~42K parts that fit a car** (39,518 fitment rows for Corolla 2018) + the JSONB `compatible_vehicles` OR-branch (`jsonb_array_elements + ILIKE`, not GIN-indexable). This is inherent to "show ALL parts for my car, cheapest first" and is **mitigated by the warmup (now primes the Redis cache, `timeout_s=120`) + 30-min cache → warm = 0s**. A full fix needs result-set reduction or a materialized per-vehicle price index — deferred (bigger architectural change, not a simple index). |

## Session — 2026-07-11 (Full run_all_tasks operational audit — zero-failure sweep)

Audited every task that had been silently erroring in `run_all_tasks` (from the logs) and the startup path. Found 7 recurring failure sources; fixed all.

| Task / area | Was failing with | Fix (verified) |
|------|------|------|
| `sync_manufacturer_registries` | `UniqueViolationError` (dup 'Gms'/'gms') every cycle | Case-insensitive `lower(btrim(name))` lookup in `_upsert_car`/`_upsert_truck` (was case-sensitive vs a case-insensitive unique index). → status=ok. |
| `sync_models_from_catalog` | `NotNullViolationError: manufacturer_id` every cycle | Resolve `vehicles.manufacturer_id` (NOT NULL FK) from car_brands (case-insensitive, name/alias, cached); skip unregistered brands. → status=ok, inserted 44. |
| `normalize_categories`, `normalize_part_types`, `dedup_catalog_parts`, `backfill_catalog_fitment_from_xls` | `DeadlockDetectedError` (batched UPDATEs on parts_catalog vs the concurrent harvester) — ~10 aborts/day total | **Central deadlock-retry in `run_all_tasks`** (retries any task up to 3× on a raised OR self-reported deadlock; timeouts not retried) + `normalize_categories` Pass 2 now uses `ORDER BY id … FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED` so it never waits on rows another writer holds. Deadlocks are transient → retry resolves them. |
| `lookup_oem_spec` | Killed at the 3600s hard timeout (status=error) 8×/day — each row is a rate-limited LLM call, `limit=500` never finished | Soft time-budget (`max_seconds=1500`): stops early and returns a clean partial `status=ok, stopped_early=True`; remaining rows next cycle. Never hits the hard timeout. |
| `_warm_search_paths` (startup) | `InterfaceError: connection is closed` every boot, on the empty-query + Toyota-Corolla fitment case | Two parts: (a) fresh DB session PER case (a shared session let one case's timeout-cancellation poison the rest); (b) that case is **78s COLD / 0s warm** (measured) — an unbounded fitment scan — but its warmup timeout was 3s, so it timed out every boot and never primed the cache → the first real customer ate the 78s. Raised the global warmup timeout 3s→25s and gave the heavy case `timeout_s=120` so the background prime actually completes. Also fixed the two warmup cases still using Hebrew category names (`מנוע`/`סינון` → `engine`/`filter`) that matched 0 rows post category root-fix. → 6/6 primed, 0 failures. **Deeper perf TODO: the 78s cold empty-query fitment scan itself should be optimized (indexing).** |

**Verified:** SearchWarmup now primes 6/6 with **0 failures** on boot; sync_* tasks return status=ok; deadlock retry + SKIP LOCKED are code-correct and will show as absence of `DeadlockDetectedError` in future cycles (monitored). Remaining external noise (ebay 403, rockauto/motorstore Playwright timeouts) is expected unavailability handled by cooldowns — not system failures.

## Session — 2026-07-10 (Recurring "Worker failed: no heartbeat" alerts — ROOT FIX)

Owner kept getting anomaly alerts: **🔴 Worker failed: run_all_tasks / run_brand_discovery — "Auto-remediated by zombie watchdog: no heartbeat within TTL"**. Root-caused and fixed so they don't recur.

| Item | Status | Summary |
|------|--------|---------|
| **JOB-02: `sync_manufacturer_registries` failed every cycle (dup 'Gms'/'gms')** | ✅ | `_upsert_car`/`_upsert_truck` (clean_manufacturers_registry.py) checked existence with case-SENSITIVE `name == 'Gms'` but the unique index `ux_car_brands_name_ci_active` is on `lower(btrim(name))` → the existing 'gms' row was missed and a duplicate INSERT attempted → `UniqueViolationError` every cycle. Fixed: case-insensitive `lower(btrim(name))` lookup (prefers active). Verified live: status=ok. |
| **JOB-03: `sync_models_from_catalog` failed every cycle (NULL manufacturer_id)** | ✅ | `vehicles.manufacturer_id` is a NOT NULL FK to `car_brands(id)` the insert never set → `NotNullViolationError` aborted the task every cycle. Fixed: resolve manufacturer→`car_brands.id` (case-insensitive, name OR alias, per-run cache) and set it; skip rows whose brand isn't registered yet rather than aborting. Verified live: status=ok, inserted 44 vehicles, skipped 0. |
| **JOB-01: restart-orphaned jobs reaped as "failed" → false owner alert (ROOT FIX)** | ✅ | **Root cause (proven):** the backend container was recreated at 08:12 (`docker inspect StartedAt`); the two "failed" jobs started at 07:27 (run_all_tasks) and 07:41 (run_brand_discovery) — i.e. in the PREVIOUS container. A restart (deploy / `compose up` recreate / OOM / crash) kills in-flight cycles mid-run; their `job_registry` rows stay `status='running'` with a frozen `last_heartbeat_at`. Nothing closed them until the db_cleanup zombie watchdog reaped them ~2h later as `failed`, which the HealthMonitor reported to the owner — even though nothing failed, the process just restarted. The `db_cleanup zombie watchdog: remediated N stale jobs` log confirms this fired on **every restart** (2026-07-08, 07-09 ×5, 07-10). **Why the existing `pre_restart.sh` layer did NOT stop it:** that script only SIGTERMs a hardcoded list of importer SUBPROCESSES (freesbe_importer, category_backfill, …) and captures worker state — but run_all_tasks / run_brand_discovery run as **asyncio tasks INSIDE the uvicorn process**, not subprocesses, so the SIGTERM loop never touched them and their job rows were never closed. **Fix — 3 layers (2026-07-10/11):** (1) **app shutdown handler** (`@app.on_event("shutdown")`) — on every GRACEFUL SIGTERM (docker restart, compose recreate) marks all `status='running'` rows `superseded` (terminal, NON-alerting) + frees their Redis locks, BEFORE the process dies. This is the primary fix — automatic, no manual script needed. (2) `_reconcile_orphaned_jobs()` at **startup** (before schedulers) — safety net for UNGRACEFUL death (OOM / SIGKILL / crash) where the shutdown handler never runs; marks previous-container orphans `superseded` + frees locks. (3) **HealthMonitor 5a** `NOT EXISTS` guard — don't alert a `failed`/`dead` job if a newer run of the same task (`split_part(job_name,':',1)`) is `running`/`completed`/`superseded`; genuine unrecovered stalls still alert. Also added the same `UPDATE … superseded` to `pre_restart.sh` (belt & suspenders). **Verified live:** inserted a `running` job, `docker restart` → `[Shutdown] closed 1 in-flight job(s) → superseded: ['run_brand_discovery']` then `[Startup] no orphaned running jobs to reconcile` (shutdown already cleaned up); ungraceful-path test earlier → `[Startup] reconciled 1 orphaned running job(s) → superseded`; 0 stale-running rows; 5a query returns 0 alertable jobs. **Rule: never mark restart-orphaned jobs `failed`; in-process asyncio jobs are closed by the shutdown handler, not by pre_restart.sh's subprocess SIGTERM.** |

## Session — 2026-07-09 (Chat-agent full-cycle test — /goal chat scenarios)

Drove real multi-turn customer conversations through the live agent brain
(`process_user_message`) as a human would — asking, buying, shipping, finance,
promotions, and emotional nuance. Found and root-fixed 9 issues. The full
"free-text car + part → fitment-verified prices → order → payment link" cycle
now works end-to-end, and the agents carry explicit human CS/sales soft-skills.

| Item | Status | Summary |
|------|--------|---------|
| **CHAT-01: free-text make+model+year never triggered search** | ✅ | Only a PLATE started the fitment flow; "מסנן שמן לטויוטה קורולה 2018" was just acknowledged, and the follow-up "כמה זה עולה?" re-asked "which part?". Fixed: deterministic `_extract_vehicle_from_text()` (make-alias map handling Hebrew prefixes ל/ב/מ/ה/ו + model lexicon + year) captures the car up front, builds a confirmed profile, and routes straight into the SAME fitment-first search — no plate required. LLM-independent so it works under 429s. |
| **CHAT-02: restated car poisoned the part query → 0 results** | ✅ | `_extract_search_query` returned the WHOLE message, so the fitment-filtered search ran on "מסנן שמן לטויוטה קורולה 2018" — the part names never contain "corolla 2018" → 0 results. Fixed: `_strip_vehicle_terms(q, make, model)` removes only the KNOWN make/model/year tokens (model-first so multi-word "מאזדה 3" strips cleanly), leaving "מסנן שמן". 0 → 5 fitment-verified results. |
| **CHAT-03: category hint returned Hebrew names the DB never uses → 0 results (ROOT FIX)** | ✅ | **Root cause:** the DB `category` column is English kebab-case slugs (`brakes`/`engine`/`filters`/`suspension-steering`… + Hebrew `כללי`) and holds ZERO Hebrew display names, but `_extract_category_hint` returned Hebrew (`בלמים`/`מנוע`/`סינון`). The filter `category ILIKE '%סינון%'` matched **0** rows (measured: `'סינון'`→0, `'filter'`→19,912; `'בלמים'`→0, `'brake'`→112,039) — every category-filtered search silently returned nothing, in BOTH languages (Hebrew never matched; English part terms weren't mapped at all). **Fix (two layers):** (1) rewrote `_CATEGORY_KEYWORDS` — ~120 bilingual keys (Hebrew AND English part terms) → English DB-category SUBSTRINGS proven to match the live vocabulary via ILIKE; `_extract_category_hint` uses word-boundary matching for Latin keys so short keys ('ac','led','oil') don't match inside unrelated words. (2) honours owner directive (KEEP make + category as the precision filter) but adds a fitment-verified fallback tier (make + `part_vehicle_fitment`, no category) for the ~2M parts dumped in the `general`/`כללי` catch-all — a category/vocab miss can never discard a part that PROVABLY fits the car; the agent verifies with the client, never shows cross-category parts silently. **Verified:** 8 part types × He+En all return results (`מסנן שמן`/`oil filter`→filter, `רפידות בלם`/`brake pads`→brake, `חיישן חמצן`/`oxygen sensor`→sensor, `מצבר`→electr, `פנס`→light, `משאבת מים`→cool). |
| **CHAT-04: multi-word Hebrew query over-constrained** | ✅ | "רפידות בלם קדמיות" → 0, but "רפידות בלם" → 5 ("Brake Pads Front") — a trailing qualifier (קדמיות) AND-matched against English catalog names kills recall. Fixed: Tier 2 progressive relaxation — drop trailing words (keep make + fitment) until a match, so the agent shows options and the customer confirms. |
| **CHAT-05: "אני רוצה להזמין" dropped to generic cart redirect** | ✅ | Free-text order intent after offers were shown fell through to the orders agent (asked for a plate again) instead of checking out the discussed part. Fixed: `_is_order_intent()` (buy/order verbs, He/En) selects the first offer → real short payment link (`/pay/XXXX`), same path as replying "1/2/3". |
| **CHAT-06: chain-of-thought / draft-options leaked to customers** | ✅ | Under 429 fallback (zai-glm/groq) the weaker model dumped raw analysis to customers: (a) several quoted candidate replies with an English meta-note "(Too vague…)"; (b) English self-analysis "The user is asking… 1. Language: Hebrew? Yes…"; (c) a good reply followed by "Let's check… the flow state mentions collect_license…". Fixed `_strip_leaked_reasoning()`: (A) collapse ≥2 quoted candidates to the first; (B) keep only the clean text BEFORE the first reasoning line (reply-then-CoT); markers broadened to catch third-person meta ("the user is…"), internal-state tokens (flow_state, collect_license…), and numbered self-checks. Falls back to a safe language-matched line if nothing clean remains. Unit-verified on all three leak samples; clean He/En replies pass untouched. |
| **CHAT-07: invented "free shipping over ₪300"** | ✅ | Agent fabricated a shipping policy. Fixed earlier this session: SHIPPING TRUTH rule — ship across Israel, exact cost/time shown at checkout per supplier, never a made-up threshold or delivery window. Verified: now says per-supplier fee at checkout. |
| **CHAT-08: professional CS/sales soft-skills not explicit** | ✅ | Added a PROFESSIONAL SKILLS & TRAITS block to the shared channel policy (`TELEGRAM_BOT_POLICY`, applies to WhatsApp/Telegram/Web for every customer agent) — operationalised (behaviours, not adjectives): active listening + memory, empathy/EQ, consultative needs-discovery, honest objection-handling/negotiation (no invented discounts), confident non-pushy closing, transparency, ownership/follow-through, adapt/personalise, human handoff, consistency. Verified live: discount question → truth-only "אין קופונים פעילים" + real-value reframe; "מעצבן" → "אני מבינה את התסכול שלך". |
| **CHAT-09: test harness didn't persist multi-turn context** | ✅ | `_chat_test.py` passed a random uuid every turn; `process_user_message` creates a NEW conversation when the id doesn't exist, so each turn was stateless (turns 3–4 "forgot" the car). Production is unaffected — the webhooks look up the conversation by chat_id and pass its real id. Fixed the harness to reuse the returned `conversation_id`. Confirmed context now persists (vehicle + pending offers carried across turns). |

**Verification (2026-07-09 live, source=web, model under heavy 429 load):**
`ask_buy` full cycle — "מסנן שמן לטויוטה קורולה 2018" → **3 fitment-verified (✅ מאומתים) Toyota oil filters ₪307/₪243/₪360** → "כמה זה עולה?" the bot **remembers the car** → "כן אני רוצה להזמין" → **real link `https://autosparefinder.co.il/pay/UwX2QDM`**. `promo_nuance` — truth-only on discounts + empathy on frustration. Leak stripper unit-verified on the 3 captured leak samples.

## Session — 2026-07-04 (Security Pentest + Code Review — 15 fixes applied)

Full code review (8 angles) + live penetration test against production container.
2 critical vulns live-confirmed and exploited in controlled test; all cleaned up.

### Security Fixes

| Item | Status | Summary |
|------|--------|---------|
| **SEC-01 CRITICAL: Unauthenticated `/api/v1/system/collect`** | ✅ | Endpoint accepted arbitrary parts from anyone — fake parts confirmed injected into production DB during test. Fixed: added `X-Collect-Secret` header check (routes/system.py). 64-char secret auto-generated, added to docker-compose.yml as `COLLECT_SECRET`, wired into harvester's `post_relay()`. Unauthenticated calls now return HTTP 403. |
| **SEC-02 CRITICAL: Google OAuth audience check disabled** | ✅ | `GOOGLE_OAUTH_CLIENT_ID` was empty in env — the conditional guard `if client_id and ...` silently skipped the audience check entirely. Any valid Google token from any app would be accepted for login. Fixed: guard inverted — empty client_id returns HTTP 500 "not configured", never bypasses the check. Verified live. **Action needed: set `GOOGLE_OAUTH_CLIENT_ID` in `.env`**. |
| **SEC-03 HIGH: Telegram admin webhook unprotected** | ✅ | `/api/v1/webhooks/telegram-admin` had no auth — anyone could POST `approve_post` to trigger TikTok publication. Confirmed HTTP 200 in live test. Fixed: added `X-Telegram-Bot-Api-Secret-Token` validation (same secret already in env). Now returns HTTP 403 without correct header. |
| **SEC-04 HIGH: Login rate limit bypassable via X-Forwarded-For** | ✅ | Rate limit used `X-Forwarded-For.split(",")[0]` which clients control. nginx uses `$proxy_add_x_forwarded_for` (appends, doesn't strip). Confirmed 5/5 requests past limit with spoofed headers. Fixed: switched to `X-Real-IP` (nginx sets to `$remote_addr`, unspoof-able). auth.py:151. |
| **SEC-05 HIGH: WhatsApp webhook — no Twilio signature validation** | ✅ (partial) | No `X-Twilio-Signature` HMAC check. Anyone could POST as any phone number, inject messages into PII DB, consume AI quota. User confirmed Twilio validation has now been connected. Telegram webhook also hardened: `if secret and ...` → `if not secret or ...` (fails closed). |
| **SEC-06 HIGH: AliExpress OAuth tokens printed to docker logs** | ✅ | Two `print(f"ALIEXPRESS_ACCESS_TOKEN=...")` lines in auth.py exposed tokens permanently in `docker logs`. Removed both prints. Tokens no longer appear in logs. |
| **SEC-07 MEDIUM: VIN rate limit return value discarded** | ✅ | `await check_rate_limit(...)` result was never checked — VIN lookup had no effective throttle. Fixed: result stored in `allowed`, raises HTTP 429 when exceeded. parts.py:3138. |

### Code Correctness Fixes

| Item | Status | Summary |
|------|--------|---------|
| **CR-01 HIGH: Inverted pricing in `task_normalize_base_price_batched`** | ✅ | Formula was `base_price = MIN(cost)` and `importer_price_ils = cost/1.45` — both backwards. `supplier_parts.price_ils` is documented as ex-VAT cost (db_update_agent.py:1243). Fixed: `base_price = cost*1.45`, `importer_price_ils = cost`, `max_price_ils = cost*1.18`. db_cleanup_agent.py:968. |
| **CR-02 HIGH: FlareSolverr dead-code guard** | ✅ | `fs_request()` swallows all exceptions and returns error dict — never raises. The `try/except` in `main()` was permanently unreachable. When FlareSolverr is down, process looped silently appearing alive to supervisor. Fixed: `fs_cleanup_stale_sessions()` now checks `r.get("status") == "ok"` and raises `RuntimeError` on failure — supervisor correctly detects and relaunches. |
| **CR-03 HIGH: Shared `state` dict race condition across threads** | ✅ | All 3 harvester worker threads mutated the same `state` dict (`total_parts +=`, `.append()`, `save_state()`) with no lock — could corrupt state file JSON. Fixed: `threading.Lock()` added at module level; all state mutations wrapped in `with _state_lock:`. |
| **CR-04 HIGH: Silent data loss on relay POST failure** | ✅ | `batch = batch[BATCH_SIZE:]` executed unconditionally even when `post_relay()` returned False — 50 parts silently discarded per failure. Fixed: slice moved inside `if` branch; on failure a warning is logged and batch is retained for retry. |
| **CR-05 HIGH: `supplier_parts` ON CONFLICT never refreshed price** | ✅ | `car_parts_ie_import_generic.py` ON CONFLICT only updated `supplier_url` — price changes and stock-out on re-harvest were silently discarded. Customers saw stale prices. Fixed: added `price_ils=EXCLUDED.price_ils, is_available=EXCLUDED.is_available` to DO UPDATE SET. |
| **CR-06 HIGH: `normalize_categories` pass-2 infinite loop** | ✅ | Pass-2 used `while True` with `updated_at=NOW()` on every batch — under continuous harvester activity rows stayed in scope perpetually, loop never broke, stalled run_all_tasks for 3600s. Fixed: `cutoff_id = MAX(id) WHERE updated_at > :since` captured at pass start; `AND id <= :cutoff_id` added to batch query. New arrivals are outside scope. db_update_agent.py:1141. |
| **CR-07 MEDIUM: Kia importer writes wrong price to `supplier_parts.price_ils`** | ✅ | Was passing `base_price` (cost×1.45, our margin price) as `price_ils`. All other IL importers write consumer reference or cost. Fixed: changed to `max_price_ils` (consumer reference price), consistent with Colmobil/Delek/MCT. import_kia_israel.py:186. |
| **CR-08 MEDIUM: Kia importer non-standard `part_type='original'`** | ✅ | `'original'` not in PART_TYPE_MAP and not healed by cleanup agent — ~30K Kia parts were invisible to `part_type='oem'` filter queries. Fixed: changed to `'oem'` (lowercase). Run one-time UPDATE to fix existing rows: `UPDATE parts_catalog SET part_type='oem' WHERE part_type='original'`. |

### Open (not fixed — require separate work)

| Item | Status | Notes |
|------|--------|-------|
| Meili incremental sync skips UUID < last_id on resume | ⚠️ Open | Parts inserted mid-run with UUID below checkpoint are permanently skipped. Low-frequency event; full rebuild recovers. Fix: decouple id-keyset and time-filter in SELECT_SQL_INCREMENTAL. |
| NULL year_from defeats fitment ON CONFLICT | ⚠️ Open | Postgres NULL ≠ NULL in unique indexes — duplicate fitment rows on re-harvest. Fix: use `COALESCE(year_from, 0)` in index or NOT NULL + sentinel value. |
| `GOOGLE_OAUTH_CLIENT_ID` not set in env | ⚠️ Action needed | Code now correctly returns 500 when unset. But Google OAuth login will be broken until the env var is set in `.env` and the container restarted. |
| WhatsApp Twilio signature validation | ✅ User-connected | User confirmed this has been wired. |
